- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: ADA diagnoses diabetes at fasting glucose 126 mg/dL or higher, confirmed.

### usmle2-082 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: A1c is 6.6 percent. There are no symptoms.
- Answer: Diabetes starts at 6.5 percent.
- Clue: Symptoms are not required.
- First-line: Confirm and treat.
- Trap: Do not call 6.6 prediabetes.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: A1c of 6.5 percent or higher diagnoses diabetes.

### usmle2-083 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Fasting glucose is 112 and A1c is 6.0 percent.
- Answer: This is prediabetes.
- Clue: Fasting 100 to 125 is the range.
- First-line: Lifestyle, then recheck.
- Trap: Do not start insulin.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Prediabetes is fasting glucose 100 to 125 mg/dL or A1c 5.7 to 6.4 percent.

### usmle2-084 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Glucose is 700, ketones are absent, and the patient is dry.
- Answer: Fluids first.
- Clue: This is the hyperosmolar state, not ketoacidosis.
- First-line: Saline, then insulin after potassium is known.
- Trap: Do not lead with insulin.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Hyperosmolar state is volume first. Insulin follows potassium.

### usmle2-085 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: A thyroid nodule is hot and TSH is suppressed.
- Answer: A hot nodule is rarely cancer.
- Clue: A cold nodule is the one that needs a needle.
- First-line: Treat the hot nodule. Biopsy the cold one.
- Trap: Do not biopsy every hot nodule.
- Source: ATA thyroid nodule guideline (2015)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Hyperfunctioning nodules are rarely malignant.

### usmle2-086 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: A solid thyroid nodule is taller than wide. TSH is normal.
- Answer: Needle it.
- Clue: Shape drives the biopsy when TSH is normal.
- First-line: Fine-needle aspiration.
- Trap: Do not watch a high-suspicion nodule for a year.
- Source: ATA thyroid nodule guideline (2015)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: High-suspicion ultrasound features warrant aspiration.

### usmle2-087 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Home pressure averages 138 over 86 on several days.
- Answer: Treat toward below 130/80.
- Clue: Out-of-office readings confirm the number.
- First-line: Lifestyle and medicine, then recheck at home.
- Trap: Do not treat one rushed office reading.
- Source: ACC/AHA high blood pressure guideline (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Most treated adults are treated to below 130/80 mm Hg.

### usmle2-088 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A patient with a prior heart attack is on no statin.
- Answer: Use a high-intensity statin.
- Clue: The expected LDL drop is about half.
- First-line: Atorvastatin 40 to 80 mg or rosuvastatin 20 to 40 mg.
- Trap: Do not start a low dose by habit.
- Source: AHA/ACC cholesterol guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Clinical atherosclerotic disease gets a high-intensity statin.

### usmle2-089 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Atrial fibrillation and a mechanical mitral valve.
- Answer: Warfarin, not a direct oral drug.
- Clue: Mechanical valves stay on warfarin.
- First-line: Warfarin, with a bridge when it is held.
- Trap: Do not use apixaban.
- Source: ACC/AHA atrial fibrillation guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: DOACs are avoided in mechanical valves and moderate-to-severe mitral stenosis.

### usmle2-090 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: New heart failure with a low ejection fraction. The patient is already on a water pill.
- Answer: Add the four disease drugs.
- Clue: The water pill only treats congestion.
- First-line: Beta blocker, ARNI or ACE, mineralocorticoid antagonist, then an SGLT2 inhibitor.
- Trap: Do not discharge on a diuretic only.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: HFrEF therapy is those four disease-modifying classes.

### usmle2-091 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Rest pain, ST depression, and a rising troponin. No ST elevation.
- Answer: Do not send this home.
- Clue: A rising troponin is injury without ST elevation.
- First-line: Antithrombotic therapy and an early invasive plan.
- Trap: Do not call it reflux.
- Source: AHA/ACC acute coronary syndrome guidance (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Non-ST-elevation ACS with a rising troponin is not discharged.

### usmle2-092 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: No pulse and a wide irregular rhythm.
- Answer: Shock first.
- Clue: Drugs do not come before the shock.
- First-line: Defibrillate, then resume compressions.
- Trap: Do not delay the shock.
- Source: AHA ACLS (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Pulseless ventricular fibrillation is defibrillated immediately.

### usmle2-093 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Pressure is falling from a large lung clot.
- Answer: Reperfuse the lung.
- Clue: Heparin alone is not enough when there is shock.
- First-line: Thrombolysis or embolectomy, then heparin.
- Trap: Do not send the patient to the ward.
- Source: ESC pulmonary embolism guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: High-risk pulmonary embolism with shock needs reperfusion.

### usmle2-094 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: COPD and a resting saturation of 86 percent.
- Answer: Oxygen is indicated at or below 88 percent.
- Clue: 90 percent is not the qualifying number.
- First-line: Prescribe oxygen, then the inhalers.
- Trap: Do not withhold oxygen because the patient quit.
- Source: GOLD report (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Long-term oxygen is for resting saturation at or below 88 percent.

### usmle2-095 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Asthma symptoms most days. The only inhaler is a rescue drug.
- Answer: Add an inhaled steroid.
- Clue: A long-acting beta agonist alone is unsafe.
- First-line: Start an inhaled corticosteroid.
- Trap: Do not refill the rescue inhaler alone.
- Source: GINA strategy (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Frequent rescue use means an inhaled corticosteroid is required.

### usmle2-096 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Pneumonia and a large pleural fluid that looks like pus.
- Answer: Drain pus.
- Clue: A small free fluid can be watched.
- First-line: Tap, then drain a complicated fluid.
- Trap: Do not leave pus on antibiotics alone.
- Source: ATS pleural guidance (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Empyema and complicated parapneumonic effusions are drained.

### usmle2-097 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: A healthy adult has outpatient pneumonia and no other disease.
- Answer: Amoxicillin, doxycycline, or a macrolide.
- Clue: Comorbidities change the regimen.
- First-line: Use the healthy-adult regimen.
- Trap: Do not start a fluoroquinolone first.
- Source: IDSA/ATS community-acquired pneumonia guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Healthy outpatients do not start with a fluoroquinolone.

### usmle2-098 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Fever, stiff neck, and a planned lumbar puncture in an hour.
- Answer: Antibiotics now.
- Clue: The puncture must not delay the drug.
- First-line: Drug first, then the puncture.
- Trap: Do not wait for the procedure.
- Source: IDSA bacterial meningitis guideline (2004)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Empiric antibiotics for suspected meningitis are immediate. The 2004 principles still govern the delay rule.

### usmle2-099 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Who needs ampicillin added for meningitis?
- Answer: Adults over 50, and weak immunity.
- Clue: The extra organism is Listeria.
- First-line: Add ampicillin to vancomycin and a cephalosporin.
- Trap: Do not use two drugs for every adult.
- Source: IDSA bacterial meningitis guideline (2004)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Listeria coverage is added after age 50 and in immunocompromised hosts.

### usmle2-100 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: A new HIV diagnosis. The CD4 count is not back.
- Answer: Start treatment now.
- Clue: The count does not gate treatment.
- First-line: Start antiretrovirals this week.
- Trap: Do not wait for a CD4 under 200.
- Source: DHHS antiretroviral guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: ART is recommended for every person with HIV as soon as possible.

### usmle2-101 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: An HIV-negative partner of a person with HIV wants prevention.
- Answer: Offer pre-exposure drugs.
- Clue: A negative test comes first.
- First-line: Pills or an injection, after a negative test.
- Trap: Do not use prevention drugs to treat HIV.
- Source: CDC PrEP guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: PrEP is for people at substantial risk, after a negative HIV test.

### usmle2-102 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: A needlestick from a person with HIV was 10 hours ago.
- Answer: Start post-exposure drugs.
- Clue: The window closes at 72 hours.
- First-line: Three drugs for 28 days.
- Trap: Do not wait for paperwork.
- Source: CDC HIV post-exposure prophylaxis (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: PEP is started as soon as possible and within 72 hours.

### usmle2-103 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: CD4 is 40 and HIV treatment starts today.
- Answer: Do not add routine MAC prophylaxis.
- Clue: Immediate treatment changed that old habit.
- First-line: Start HIV treatment, and pneumocystis prophylaxis while CD4 is under 200.
- Trap: Do not delay HIV treatment.
- Source: NIH opportunistic infection guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Primary MAC prophylaxis is not routine when ART starts immediately.

### usmle2-104 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: CD4 is 80, oxygen is low, and the film is diffuse.
- Answer: Add steroids if oxygen is poor.
- Clue: The pneumonia is pneumocystis until proven otherwise.
- First-line: Trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole, plus steroids if PaO2 is under 70 mm Hg.
- Trap: Do not steroid every mild case.
- Source: NIH pneumocystis guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Steroids are for moderate-to-severe PCP, PaO2 under 70 mm Hg or A-a gradient over 35.

### usmle2-105 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Reproductive system
- Front: Purulent urethral discharge. Weight is 80 kg.
- Answer: Ceftriaxone 500 mg once.
- Clue: Add doxycycline unless chlamydia is excluded.
- First-line: One intramuscular dose.
- Trap: Do not use oral cefixime first.
- Source: CDC STI treatment guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Uncomplicated gonorrhea is ceftriaxone 500 mg once if weight is under 150 kg.

### usmle2-106 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Reproductive system
- Front: Confirmed chlamydia.
- Answer: Doxycycline for 7 days.
- Clue: A single azithromycin dose is no longer first.
- First-line: Doxycycline 100 mg twice daily for 7 days.
- Trap: Do not lead with azithromycin.
- Source: CDC STI treatment guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: CDC prefers 7 days of doxycycline for chlamydia.

### usmle2-107 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Reproductive system
- Front: A painless genital ulcer. RPR is positive. No neurologic signs.
- Answer: One penicillin shot.
- Clue: Early disease is a single intramuscular dose.
- First-line: Benzathine penicillin 2.4 million units once.
- Trap: Do not use oral penicillin.
- Source: CDC STI treatment guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Early syphilis is one dose of benzathine penicillin. Neurosyphilis needs intravenous penicillin.

### usmle2-108 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Reproductive system
- Front: Pelvic pain and cervical motion tenderness.
- Answer: Cover gonorrhea, chlamydia, and anaerobes.
- Clue: Pregnancy or vomiting means admission.
- First-line: Ceftriaxone, doxycycline, and metronidazole.
- Trap: Do not use one drug.
- Source: CDC STI treatment guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: PID regimens cover all three. Pregnancy and abscess are admitted.

### usmle2-109 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Burning urine for one day in a healthy woman. No fever.
- Answer: Nitrofurantoin for 5 days.
- Clue: Three days of sulfa or one fosfomycin dose also qualify.
- First-line: Use one of those three.
- Trap: Do not start a fluoroquinolone.
- Source: IDSA uncomplicated cystitis guideline (2011)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: First-line cystitis drugs are nitrofurantoin, trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole, or fosfomycin.

### usmle2-110 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Fever, flank pain, and vomiting.
- Answer: This is not cystitis.
- Clue: Nitrofurantoin does not reach the kidney.
- First-line: Intravenous therapy until she can drink.
- Trap: Do not send nitrofurantoin.
- Source: IDSA pyelonephritis guidance (2011)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Pyelonephritis needs a drug with kidney levels. Nitrofurantoin fails that test.

### usmle2-111 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Skin and subcutaneous tissue
- Front: A diabetic ulcer probes to bone.
- Answer: That is bone infection.
- Clue: Probe-to-bone is a bedside test.
- First-line: Image and treat the bone, then offload.
- Trap: Do not use two weeks of pills.
- Source: IDSA diabetic foot guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Probe-to-bone suggests osteomyelitis and needs more than a short antibiotic course.

### usmle2-112 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: Drug-susceptible active tuberculosis.
- Answer: Four drugs, then two.
- Clue: Two months of four, then two drugs to finish six months.
- First-line: RIPE, then isoniazid and rifampin.
- Trap: Do not use a latent regimen.
- Source: CDC tuberculosis treatment (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Active drug-susceptible TB is 6 months, with an intensive 2-month phase.

### usmle2-113 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Hepatitis B DNA is high and ALT is twice normal for years.
- Answer: Treat immune-active disease.
- Clue: Inactive carriers are not all treated.
- First-line: Start a high-barrier antiviral.
- Trap: Do not ignore a very high viral load.
- Source: AASLD hepatitis B guidance (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Immune-active hepatitis B is treated. Inactive infection is monitored.

### usmle2-114 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Chronic hepatitis C and no symptoms.
- Answer: Treat now.
- Clue: Symptoms are not the indication.
- First-line: A short direct-acting course.
- Trap: Do not wait for cirrhosis.
- Source: AASLD/IDSA hepatitis C guidance (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Nearly all chronic hepatitis C is treated.

### usmle2-115 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: The serum-ascites albumin gradient is 1.4.
- Answer: Portal hypertension.
- Clue: 1.1 or higher is portal. Under 1.1 is peritoneal.
- First-line: Treat the portal pressure.
- Trap: Do not start tuberculosis drugs for a high gradient.
- Source: AASLD ascites guidance (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: SAAG of 1.1 g/dL or higher means portal hypertension.

### usmle2-116 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: A variceal bleed has stopped. The patient is stable.
- Answer: Band, then a beta blocker.
- Clue: Do not start the blocker while pressure is low.
- First-line: Nonselective beta blocker after stabilization.
- Trap: Do not use banding as the only plan.
- Source: AASLD portal hypertension guidance (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Banding plus a nonselective beta blocker prevents recurrent variceal bleeding.

### usmle2-117 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: New iron deficiency at age 55.
- Answer: Colonoscopy, not iron alone.
- Clue: Iron can hide a cancer if it is the only plan.
- First-line: Scope, and give iron.
- Trap: Do not recheck the blood count and stop.
- Source: ACG colorectal cancer screening (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: New iron deficiency in this age warrants colonoscopy.

### usmle2-118 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Burning epigastric pain. Age 32. No alarm symptom.
- Answer: Test for the stomach organism or a short acid blocker.
- Clue: Age 60 or alarms mean a scope.
- First-line: Noninvasive test, then treat if positive.
- Trap: Do not scope every young patient.
- Source: ACG dyspepsia guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Under 60 without alarms: test-and-treat or an acid blocker. Endoscopy is for age 60 or alarms.

### usmle2-119 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Bloody diarrhea and fever after travel.
- Answer: Do not give an antimotility drug.
- Clue: Hydration is the base.
- First-line: Azithromycin if it is severe.
- Trap: Do not use loperamide alone.
- Source: IDSA infectious diarrhea guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Antimotility drugs are avoided in bloody or febrile diarrhea.

### usmle2-120 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: eGFR is 25 and urine albumin is high.
- Answer: ACE or ARB, plus an SGLT2 inhibitor.
- Clue: Do not combine an ACE and an ARB.
- First-line: Start both indicated classes.
- Trap: Do not stop the ACE for a small creatinine bump.
- Source: KDIGO chronic kidney disease guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Albuminuric CKD gets a RAS inhibitor and an SGLT2 inhibitor.

### usmle2-121 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Potassium is 6.8 with peaked T waves.
- Answer: Calcium first.
- Clue: Calcium does not lower the potassium.
- First-line: Calcium, then insulin with glucose.
- Trap: Do not start with a binding resin alone.
- Source: AHA hyperkalemia emergency care (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Membrane stabilization with calcium precedes shifting potassium.

### usmle2-122 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Sodium is 118 and the patient is seizing.
- Answer: Hypertonic saline now.
- Clue: Stop the seizure. Do not normalize the sodium in an hour.
- First-line: A bolus of 3 percent, then slow correction.
- Trap: Do not overcorrect.
- Source: European hyponatremia guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Seizures from hyponatremia are treated with hypertonic saline boluses.

### usmle2-123 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: A 4 mm distal ureteral stone. No fever.
- Answer: It can pass.
- Clue: Stones of 5 mm or less usually pass.
- First-line: Pain control and a strainer.
- Trap: Do not stent a tiny sterile stone.
- Source: AUA ureteral stone guideline (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Most stones 5 mm or smaller pass. Fever means drain, not passage.

### usmle2-124 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Last known well was 2 hours ago. CT shows no blood. The deficit is disabling.
- Answer: Thrombolysis is available inside 4.5 hours.
- Clue: Selected patients can still have clot retrieval up to 24 hours.
- First-line: Call stroke.
- Trap: Do not say nothing can be done after 4.5 hours.
- Source: AHA/ASA early stroke management (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: IV thrombolysis is within 4.5 hours. Thrombectomy can extend to 24 hours.

### usmle2-125 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Thunderclap headache. CT at 6 hours is negative. The story is classic.
- Answer: Do the lumbar puncture.
- Clue: A negative CT does not finish a classic story.
- First-line: Puncture, then a vessel study if positive.
- Trap: Do not discharge on the CT.
- Source: AHA/ASA subarachnoid hemorrhage guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Persistent suspicion after a negative CT goes to lumbar puncture.

### usmle2-126 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: One uncomplicated seizure yesterday. Exam and sodium are normal.
- Answer: Do not start a lifelong drug tonight.
- Clue: The electroencephalogram and imaging still matter.
- First-line: Counsel and arrange the workup.
- Trap: Do not load a chronic drug by reflex.
- Source: AAN first seizure guideline (2015)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: A single unprovoked seizure does not automatically need long-term medication.

### usmle2-127 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Migraine days are frequent and the patient misses work.
- Answer: Start prevention.
- Clue: Four or more days a month is a common point to discuss it.
- First-line: A preventive plus a fitting acute drug.
- Trap: Do not use an opioid as the plan.
- Source: AHS migraine consensus (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Frequent or disabling migraine gets prevention, not only acute drugs.

### usmle2-128 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: A rest tremor improves when the hand is used. Another tremor appears when the hands are held out.
- Answer: Rest tremor vs action tremor.
- Clue: Rest is the dopamine disease. Action is essential tremor.
- First-line: Levodopa for rest. A beta blocker for essential tremor.
- Trap: Do not give levodopa for every shake.
- Source: AAN essential tremor guideline (2011)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Essential tremor is an action tremor. Parkinson tremor is a rest tremor.

### usmle2-129 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Brief electric face pain when chewing. The exam is normal.
- Answer: Carbamazepine.
- Clue: Image if the patient is young or the exam is abnormal.
- First-line: Start carbamazepine.
- Trap: Do not start an opioid.
- Source: AAN trigeminal neuralgia guideline (2008)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Carbamazepine is first-line for classic trigeminal neuralgia.

### usmle2-130 · Musculoskeletal

- Outline: Musculoskeletal system
- Front: Fever and a tender spine after injection-drug use. Strength is normal.
- Answer: MRI tonight.
- Clue: Weakness is late.
- First-line: Image and antibiotics after cultures.
- Trap: Do not call this a strain.
- Source: IDSA vertebral osteomyelitis guideline (2015)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Fever plus focal back pain needs urgent MRI for abscess or discitis.

### usmle2-131 · Musculoskeletal

- Outline: Musculoskeletal system
- Front: A swollen calf after a flight. The clot score is high.
- Answer: Ultrasound, not a D-dimer.
- Clue: A high probability skips the D-dimer.
- First-line: Image, then anticoagulate a clot.
- Trap: Do not clear a high-probability leg with a D-dimer.
- Source: ASH venous thromboembolism guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: High pretest probability goes straight to ultrasound.

### usmle2-132 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: Hemoglobin is 7.1. The patient is stable and not bleeding.
- Answer: Transfuse under 7 g/dL.
- Clue: Active coronary disease uses about 8 g/dL.
- First-line: One unit, then recheck.
- Trap: Do not transfuse to 10 by habit.
- Source: AABB transfusion guideline (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: The restrictive threshold is 7 g/dL. Acute coronary syndrome uses 8 g/dL.

### usmle2-133 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: Fever and neutrophils under 500.
- Answer: Antibiotics within the hour.
- Clue: This is febrile neutropenia.
- First-line: Broad intravenous antibiotics now.
- Trap: Do not wait for a recount.
- Source: IDSA febrile neutropenia guideline (2010)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Fever plus ANC under 500 gets immediate empiric antibiotics.

### usmle2-134 · Reproductive

- Outline: Pregnancy, childbirth, and the puerperium
- Front: 34 weeks, pressure 168 over 112, and a headache.
- Answer: Treat the pressure and deliver.
- Clue: 160 systolic or 110 diastolic is severe-range.
- First-line: Intravenous treatment, magnesium, then delivery.
- Trap: Do not send her home.
- Source: ACOG preeclampsia guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Severe-range pressures are treated immediately. Delivery is indicated at 34 weeks or later for severe preeclampsia.

### usmle2-135 · Reproductive

- Outline: Pregnancy, childbirth, and the puerperium
- Front: Painless third-trimester bleeding. The placenta covers the cervix.
- Answer: No digital exam.
- Clue: Painful rigid bleeding is abruption, a different path.
- First-line: Ultrasound and resuscitation.
- Trap: Do not examine the cervix with a hand.
- Source: ACOG placenta previa guidance (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Suspected previa: no digital exam.

### usmle2-136 · Reproductive

- Outline: Pregnancy, childbirth, and the puerperium
- Front: The fetal heart rate stays down and variability is gone.
- Answer: Deliver.
- Clue: Not every deceleration is an emergency. This pattern is.
- First-line: Intrauterine resuscitation and delivery.
- Trap: Do not keep watching.
- Source: ACOG intrapartum fetal surveillance (2009)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Absent variability with recurrent late decelerations is a category III tracing.

### usmle2-137 · Reproductive

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: First prenatal visit. Which labs are drawn now?
- Answer: HIV, syphilis, hepatitis B, blood type, and a blood count.
- Clue: The diabetes screen is at 24 to 28 weeks, not today.
- First-line: Draw the first-visit panel.
- Trap: Do not wait until 28 weeks for HIV.
- Source: ACOG prenatal care (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: The initial panel includes HIV, syphilis, hepatitis B, type and screen, and CBC.

### usmle2-138 · Reproductive

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: Heavy periods. She wants to keep the uterus.
- Answer: A levonorgestrel device is first-line.
- Clue: Surgery is for failure of medicine.
- First-line: Offer the device.
- Trap: Do not start with hysterectomy.
- Source: ACOG abnormal uterine bleeding (2013)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: A levonorgestrel IUD is first-line medical therapy for heavy menstrual bleeding.

### usmle2-139 · Reproductive

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: A mobile breast lump at age 28.
- Answer: Ultrasound first.
- Clue: Mammogram is for older patients or a suspicious ultrasound.
- First-line: Image, then biopsy a solid mass.
- Trap: Do not reassure a solid mass.
- Source: ACR breast imaging (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Under age 30, ultrasound is the first test for a palpable mass.

### usmle2-140 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: A 3-week-old has a fever of 38.4.
- Answer: Full evaluation and admission.
- Clue: This age does not go home.
- First-line: Cultures and antibiotics.
- Trap: Do not use oral antibiotics.
- Source: AAP febrile infant guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Febrile infants in the first weeks are evaluated for invasive infection and admitted.

### usmle2-141 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: A 6-month-old is wheezing, feeding, and saturating 97 percent.
- Answer: Support only.
- Clue: This is bronchiolitis.
- First-line: Suction and feeding.
- Trap: Do not start steroids or antibiotics.
- Source: AAP bronchiolitis guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Routine albuterol, steroids, and antibiotics are not recommended.

### usmle2-142 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Six days of fever, red eyes, rash, and swollen hands in a 4-year-old.
- Answer: Immunoglobulin and aspirin.
- Clue: Coronary arteries are the stake.
- First-line: IVIG and aspirin, then an echocardiogram.
- Trap: Do not send the child home.
- Source: AHA Kawasaki disease guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: IVIG and aspirin reduce coronary aneurysms.

### usmle2-143 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Bilious vomiting at 3 weeks of age.
- Answer: A twist until proven otherwise.
- Clue: Nonbilious projectile vomiting is the pylorus, a different disease.
- First-line: Stop feeds and image.
- Trap: Do not give an acid blocker.
- Source: AAP surgical referral principles (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Bilious emesis in an infant is malrotation with volvulus until excluded.

### usmle2-144 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: A newborn screen flags low thyroid. The baby looks well.
- Answer: Start levothyroxine now.
- Clue: Symptoms come too late.
- First-line: Treat, then confirm the serum tests.
- Trap: Do not wait a month.
- Source: AAP congenital hypothyroidism (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Treatment starts immediately to protect development.

### usmle2-145 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Barky cough and stridor at rest in a toddler.
- Answer: Steroid, plus nebulized epinephrine.
- Clue: A bark without rest stridor can be a steroid alone.
- First-line: Dexamethasone for all. Epinephrine if stridor is at rest.
- Trap: Do not discharge rest stridor without a watch.
- Source: AAP croup guidance (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Epinephrine is added for stridor at rest, then the child is observed.

### usmle2-146 · Dermatologic

- Outline: Skin and subcutaneous tissue
- Front: A small golden crust on a child's face. The child is well.
- Answer: Topical mupirocin.
- Clue: Widespread disease needs an oral drug.
- First-line: Mupirocin for a small area.
- Trap: Do not start intravenous drugs.
- Source: IDSA skin infection guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Limited impetigo is topical mupirocin.

### usmle2-147 · Psychiatry

- Outline: Behavioral health
- Front: Rigid muscles, high fever, and a high creatine kinase on an antipsychotic. A different patient has clonus and diarrhea on an antidepressant.
- Answer: Rigidity vs clonus.
- Clue: Rigidity is the neuroleptic reaction. Clonus is serotonin.
- First-line: Stop the drug. The antidotes are not interchangeable.
- Trap: Do not swap the treatments.
- Source: APA emergency psychiatry (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: NMS is rigidity and high CK. Serotonin syndrome is clonus.

### usmle2-148 · Psychiatry

- Outline: Behavioral health
- Front: Worthlessness, 4 a.m. waking, and no work for six weeks.
- Answer: Treat the depression.
- Clue: This is not ordinary grief.
- First-line: Start treatment and ask about suicide.
- Trap: Do not watch it as mourning.
- Source: APA depression guideline (2010)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Functional loss and worthlessness define a depressive episode that is treated.

### usmle2-149 · Prevention

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: A 26-year-old has never had a cervical screen.
- Answer: Cytology every 3 years from 21 to 29.
- Clue: HPV testing is not routine under 30.
- First-line: Start cytology.
- Trap: Do not screen under 21.
- Source: USPSTF cervical cancer screening (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Ages 21 to 29: cytology every 3 years. Ages 30 to 65: primary HPV every 5 years is an option.

### usmle2-150 · Prevention

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: A 40-year-old has never been tested for HIV or hepatitis C.
- Answer: Test once for both.
- Clue: HIV once from 15 to 65. Hepatitis C once from 18 to 79.
- First-line: Offer both today.
- Trap: Do not wait for a risk story.
- Source: USPSTF HIV and hepatitis C screening (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: One-time HIV and hepatitis C screening is recommended in these age ranges.

### usmle2-151 · Emergency

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: A child drank antifreeze. The osmolar gap is high.
- Answer: Fomepizole now.
- Clue: Do not wait for kidney failure.
- First-line: Fomepizole, then dialysis if severe.
- Trap: Do not watch overnight.
- Source: ACMT toxic alcohol guidance (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Fomepizole blocks alcohol dehydrogenase for methanol and ethylene glycol.

### usmle2-152 · Emergency

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A regular wide-complex tachycardia. Pressure is normal.
- Answer: Treat it as ventricular tachycardia.
- Clue: Do not assume it is supraventricular.
- First-line: Procainamide or a shock.
- Trap: Do not give verapamil.
- Source: AHA tachycardia algorithm (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Regular wide-complex tachycardia is treated as ventricular tachycardia.

### usmle2-153 · Emergency

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: No breath sounds, neck veins up, and the trachea is shifting.
- Answer: Decompress before a film.
- Clue: The clinical picture is the diagnosis.
- First-line: Needle or finger, then a chest tube.
- Trap: Do not go to CT.
- Source: ATLS tension pneumothorax (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Tension pneumothorax is treated before imaging.

### usmle2-154 · Ophthalmology

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Halos, a mid-dilated pupil, and a rock-hard eye.
- Answer: Angle closure. Treat tonight.
- Clue: Do not dilate the eye.
- First-line: Pressure drops, then a laser iridotomy.
- Trap: Do not send the patient to clinic next week.
- Source: AAO acute angle closure (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Acute angle closure is an emergency: lower the pressure and perform iridotomy.

### usmle2-155 · Ophthalmology

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: A contact-lens user has a white corneal spot and severe pain.
- Answer: Ulcer until proven otherwise.
- Clue: Steroid drops are dangerous early.
- First-line: Same-day eye care. Stop the lens.
- Trap: Do not patch it.
- Source: AAO bacterial keratitis (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Contact-lens keratitis is urgent. Steroids wait until the infection is controlled.

### usmle2-156 · Ethics

- Outline: Behavioral health
- Front: A capable patient refuses surgery. The family insists.
- Answer: The refusal stands.
- Clue: A bad choice is not automatically incapacity.
- First-line: Document and honor it.
- Trap: Do not operate because the family insists.
- Source: AMA Code of Medical Ethics on consent (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: A capable refusal is honored.

### usmle2-157 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Black stool, dizziness on standing, and a falling hemoglobin.
- Answer: Resuscitate, then urgent endoscopy.
- Clue: Orthostatic symptoms mean a large bleed.
- First-line: Two lines, blood, and a scope.
- Trap: Do not schedule an outpatient stool card.
- Source: ACG upper GI bleeding guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Unstable upper bleeding is resuscitated and endoscoped urgently.

### usmle2-158 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A thigh-vein clot after hip surgery.
- Answer: Treat for 3 months.
- Clue: A surgical provoke is not lifelong therapy.
- First-line: Three months, then stop if the provoke is gone.
- Trap: Do not commit every provoked clot to life.
- Source: ASH venous thromboembolism guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Surgically provoked VTE is treated for 3 months.

### usmle2-159 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A second unprovoked clot.
- Answer: Discuss extended therapy.
- Clue: The 3-month surgical rule does not apply.
- First-line: Extend anticoagulation if bleeding risk allows.
- Trap: Do not stop at 3 months without that talk.
- Source: ASH venous thromboembolism guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Unprovoked recurrent VTE is considered for extended therapy.

### usmle2-160 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Skin and subcutaneous tissue
- Front: A small well cellulitis with no pus.
- Answer: An oral drug aimed at streptococci.
- Clue: Pus changes the target toward resistant staph.
- First-line: Cephalexin.
- Trap: Do not start two intravenous drugs.
- Source: IDSA skin and soft tissue guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Nonpurulent cellulitis targets streptococci.

### usmle2-161 · Endocrine

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: Irregular periods and acne. TSH and prolactin are normal. She wants pregnancy.
- Answer: Letrozole, not clomiphene first.
- Clue: Two of three: irregular cycles, high androgens, or polycystic ovaries.
- First-line: Letrozole for ovulation.
- Trap: Do not start injectable gonadotropins.
- Source: Endocrine Society PCOS guideline (2013)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Letrozole is first-line ovulation induction in PCOS.

### usmle2-162 · Reproductive

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: Vaginal bleeding at age 62. She is not on hormones.
- Answer: Sample the lining.
- Clue: Any postmenopausal bleeding is investigated.
- First-line: Ultrasound and biopsy.
- Trap: Do not watch for three cycles.
- Source: ACOG postmenopausal bleeding (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Postmenopausal bleeding requires endometrial assessment.

### usmle2-163 · Oncology

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: A mammogram is BI-RADS 4.
- Answer: Biopsy.
- Clue: Category 3 is the short follow-up. Category 4 is tissue.
- First-line: Tissue diagnosis.
- Trap: Do not repeat in 6 months.
- Source: ACR BI-RADS (2013)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: BI-RADS 4 requires biopsy.

### usmle2-164 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: An 8 mm solid lung nodule in a 60-year-old smoker. No old films.
- Answer: Follow it. Do not ignore it.
- Clue: Size and risk set the interval.
- First-line: CT on the nodule interval.
- Trap: Do not resect today and do not shrug.
- Source: Fleischner Society nodule guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Solid nodules around 8 mm are followed. They are not dismissed.

### usmle2-165 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: INR is 5.2. There is no bleeding.
- Answer: Hold doses. No routine vitamin K.
- Clue: Vitamin K is for an INR over 10 or for bleeding.
- First-line: Hold and lower the weekly dose.
- Trap: Do not give plasma for a number.
- Source: ASH warfarin reversal guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: INR 4.5 to 10 without bleeding: no routine vitamin K.

### usmle2-166 · Emergency

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: A toddler swallowed a button battery. It is in the esophagus.
- Answer: Remove it now.
- Clue: It burns through in hours.
- First-line: Emergent endoscopy.
- Trap: Do not wait for it to pass.
- Source: NASPGHAN button battery guideline (2015)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: An esophageal button battery is removed emergently.

### usmle2-167 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Crohn disease that flares every time steroids stop.
- Answer: Steroids do not maintain remission.
- Clue: They induce. A steroid-sparing drug maintains.
- First-line: Start maintenance, then taper the steroid.
- Trap: Do not leave prednisone for a year.
- Source: ACG Crohn disease guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Corticosteroids are not maintenance therapy.

### usmle2-168 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Day 3 after a coronary stent for a heart attack.
- Answer: Two antiplatelet drugs for 6 to 12 months.
- Clue: Aspirin continues after that.
- First-line: Continue both for the window.
- Trap: Do not stop both for a dental cleaning.
- Source: ACC/AHA coronary revascularization guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Dual therapy after ACS is usually 6 to 12 months.

### usmle2-169 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Polycystic kidneys and a parent who ruptured a brain aneurysm.
- Answer: Screen for an aneurysm.
- Clue: Not every patient with the kidney disease is screened.
- First-line: Vessel imaging because of the family rupture.
- Trap: Do not ignore that history.
- Source: ASN polycystic kidney guidance (2015)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: A family history of aneurysm rupture is an indication to screen in ADPKD.

### usmle2-170 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Severe asthma in the emergency department. The patient speaks in words. Saturation is 91 percent.
- Answer: Continuous nebulizer, steroid, and magnesium.
- Clue: Words, not sentences, mean severe.
- First-line: Treat and admit if there is little response.
- Trap: Do not discharge after one treatment.
- Source: GINA strategy (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Severe exacerbations get oxygen, repeated bronchodilators, steroids, and magnesium.

### usmle2-171 · Musculoskeletal

- Outline: Musculoskeletal system
- Front: A dirty puncture through a shoe. The last tetanus shot was 12 years ago.
- Answer: Vaccine, and immunoglobulin if the series is incomplete.
- Clue: A dirty wound and a long interval needs the vaccine.
- First-line: Clean and update tetanus.
- Trap: Do not skip immunoglobulin when the series is unknown.
- Source: CDC tetanus wound management (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Dirty wounds in underimmunized patients get vaccine and immunoglobulin.

### usmle2-172 · Dermatologic

- Outline: Skin and subcutaneous tissue
- Front: A melanoma is 0.4 mm thick and not ulcerated.
- Answer: Excise with a 1 cm margin.
- Clue: Node biopsy is for higher-risk thickness.
- First-line: One-centimeter margin.
- Trap: Do not use a 2 cm margin.
- Source: AAD melanoma guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Thin melanoma without ulceration is a 1 cm margin.

### usmle2-173 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: A child with immune thrombocytopenia has wet purpura.
- Answer: Treat mucosal bleeding.
- Clue: Skin petechiae alone can be watched.
- First-line: Steroids or immunoglobulin.
- Trap: Do not observe mouth bleeding.
- Source: ASH immune thrombocytopenia guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Mucosal bleeding in childhood ITP is treated.

### usmle2-174 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Type 2 diabetes, heart failure, and metformin only. A1c is 7.4.
- Answer: Add an SGLT2 inhibitor.
- Clue: Heart failure is the indication, not the A1c.
- First-line: Start it.
- Trap: Do not add a sulfonylurea for the heart.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: SGLT2 inhibitors are recommended in type 2 diabetes with heart failure.

### usmle2-175 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Type 2 diabetes and a prior heart attack. No heart failure.
- Answer: Add a GLP-1 drug or an SGLT2 inhibitor with a proven benefit.
- Clue: The outcome drug matters more than the cheapest add-on.
- First-line: Start one.
- Trap: Do not decide from the A1c alone.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Established atherosclerotic disease gets an agent with proven cardiovascular benefit.

### usmle2-176 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Insulin glargine is ordered as zero because the patient is NPO for a scope.
- Answer: Give most of the basal dose.
- Clue: Mealtime insulin is held. Basal is not.
- First-line: About 80 percent of basal, and glucose checks.
- Trap: Do not hold all insulin.
- Source: ADA hospital diabetes care (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Basal insulin continues, usually reduced, when a patient is NPO.

### usmle2-177 · Reproductive

- Outline: Pregnancy, childbirth, and the puerperium
- Front: A normal pregnancy reaches 41 weeks.
- Answer: Offer induction.
- Clue: Past 42 weeks the stillbirth risk rises further.
- First-line: Offer induction at 41 weeks.
- Trap: Do not wait until 43.
- Source: ACOG late-term pregnancy (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Induction is offered at 41 weeks.

### usmle2-178 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Infectious Diseases
- Front: A well 2-month-old is due for vaccines.
- Answer: DTaP, Hib, pneumococcal, polio, and rotavirus.
- Clue: Measles vaccine is not due.
- First-line: Give the 2-month series.
- Trap: Do not delay for a mild cold.
- Source: CDC child immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: MMR starts at 12 months, not at 2 months.

### usmle2-179 · Oncology

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: A smoker has a low sodium, concentrated urine, and a lung mass.
- Answer: Small cell lung cancer until proven otherwise.
- Clue: The sodium picture is the hormone syndrome.
- First-line: Fluid-restrict and stage the cancer.
- Trap: Do not give saline as the only plan.
- Source: NCCN small cell lung cancer (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Small cell lung cancer is the classic solid tumor behind SIADH.

### usmle2-180 · Oncology

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: A cavitary lung mass, calcium of 12, and a low PTH.
- Answer: Squamous cell lung cancer.
- Clue: The calcium peptide suppresses PTH.
- First-line: Fluids and tissue.
- Trap: Do not send the patient for parathyroid surgery.
- Source: NCCN non-small cell lung cancer (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: PTHrP from squamous cell carcinoma suppresses PTH.

### usmle2-181 · Oncology

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: Night sweats and a mediastinal mass in a young adult.
- Answer: Excisional biopsy.
- Clue: A needle alone can miss the architecture.
- First-line: Excise a node before steroids if you can.
- Trap: Do not start steroids first.
- Source: NCCN lymphoma principles (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Suspected lymphoma needs an excisional biopsy. Steroids can obscure it.

### usmle2-182 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Creatinine rose from 1.0 to 1.3 after an ACE inhibitor in heart failure.
- Answer: Keep the ACE inhibitor.
- Clue: A small rise is expected.
- First-line: Continue it.
- Trap: Do not stop it for a 0.3 rise.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: A creatinine rise of about 30 percent is accepted after a RAS inhibitor.

### usmle2-183 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Symptomatic COPD. Resting saturation is 90 percent.
- Answer: A long-acting bronchodilator, not oxygen.
- Clue: Oxygen starts at or below 88 percent.
- First-line: Start the long-acting inhaler.
- Trap: Do not prescribe oxygen for 90 percent.
- Source: GOLD report (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Long-acting bronchodilators are first. Oxygen is for saturation at or below 88 percent.

### usmle2-184 · Prevention

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: A 66-year-old asks what is due besides the flu shot.
- Answer: Shingles series and a pneumococcal conjugate vaccine if missing.
- Clue: Shingles starts at age 50, two doses.
- First-line: Give what is missing.
- Trap: Do not skip shingles because of old chickenpox.
- Source: CDC adult immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Recombinant zoster vaccine and pneumococcal conjugate vaccine are adult schedule vaccines.

### usmle2-185 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Fever and a liver abscess after travel.
- Answer: Metronidazole if it is amebic. Drain if it is pyogenic.
- Clue: A luminal agent follows metronidazole for ameba.
- First-line: Treat the organism and drain pus.
- Trap: Do not operate every abscess the day it is seen.
- Source: IDSA intra-abdominal infection guideline (2010)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Amebic abscess is metronidazole plus a luminal agent. Pyogenic abscesses are drained.

### usmle2-186 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: An 86-year-old with many illnesses has an A1c goal under 6.5.
- Answer: Loosen the goal.
- Clue: Tight control causes hypoglycemia.
- First-line: A goal near 8 percent is often safer.
- Trap: Do not chase 6.5.
- Source: ADA older-adult diabetes guidance (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Complex older adults often use an A1c goal under 8 percent.

### usmle2-187 · Prevention

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A 68-year-old man who once smoked asks about an aneurysm screen.
- Answer: One ultrasound.
- Clue: The window is 65 to 75.
- First-line: One study.
- Trap: Do not repeat it yearly.
- Source: USPSTF abdominal aortic aneurysm screening (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: One-time ultrasound for men 65 to 75 who have ever smoked.

### usmle2-188 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Weakness for 30 minutes. Known atrial fibrillation. CT shows no blood. Not anticoagulated.
- Answer: This can still be a lytic case.
- Clue: Fibrillation is not itself a ban.
- First-line: Reperfuse if the checklist is met.
- Trap: Do not anticoagulate instead of reperfusing.
- Source: AHA/ASA early stroke management (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Atrial fibrillation does not forbid thrombolysis when criteria are met.

### usmle2-189 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: Platelets are 8,000 before a needed central line. The patient has immune thrombocytopenia and is not bleeding.
- Answer: Transfuse for the procedure, not to a normal count.
- Clue: Stable ITP without bleeding is not transfused for the number.
- First-line: Match the product to the line.
- Trap: Do not chase a normal count.
- Source: ASH immune thrombocytopenia guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Platelets in ITP are for bleeding or a procedure.

### usmle2-190 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Random glucose is 240 with thirst and weight loss.
- Answer: Diabetes. One symptomatic value is enough.
- Clue: A second test is for patients without symptoms.
- First-line: Treat now.
- Trap: Do not wait for a second A1c.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Symptoms plus glucose of 200 mg/dL or higher diagnose diabetes.

### usmle2-191 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Low TSH and high free T4 in the first trimester.
- Answer: PTU now, methimazole later.
- Clue: Methimazole is avoided in the first trimester.
- First-line: Switch after the first trimester.
- Trap: Do not use radioactive iodine.
- Source: ATA thyroid and pregnancy (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: PTU is preferred in the first trimester of pregnancy.

### usmle2-192 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A new harsh murmur weeks after a heart attack.
- Answer: A mechanical complication.
- Clue: Septal rupture or a broken papillary muscle.
- First-line: Urgent echo and surgery.
- Trap: Do not add a pill and wait.
- Source: AHA mechanical complication care (2013)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Postinfarction mechanical complications need urgent surgery.

### usmle2-193 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Pressure is 90 and the rhythm is a slow wide regular beat.
- Answer: Pace.
- Clue: Unstable bradycardia is electrical.
- First-line: Transcutaneous pacing.
- Trap: Do not rely on an oral drug.
- Source: AHA bradycardia algorithm (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Unstable bradycardia is paced.

### usmle2-194 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Pleural fluid pH is 7.10 and organisms are seen.
- Answer: Drain it.
- Clue: pH under 7.20 with infection is complicated.
- First-line: A chest tube.
- Trap: Do not retap next week.
- Source: ATS pleural guidance (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Infected fluid with pH under 7.20 is drained.

### usmle2-195 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: CD4 is 150 and there is no pneumocystis prophylaxis.
- Answer: Start prophylaxis under 200.
- Clue: Trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole is the drug.
- First-line: Start it and start HIV treatment.
- Trap: Do not wait for thrush.
- Source: NIH opportunistic infection guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: PCP prophylaxis begins at CD4 under 200.

### usmle2-196 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Immune system
- Front: CD4 is 80 and toxoplasma IgG is positive.
- Answer: The sulfa drug covers both threats.
- Clue: Toxoplasma prophylaxis starts under 100.
- First-line: Trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole.
- Trap: Do not add a second drug if sulfa is on.
- Source: NIH opportunistic infection guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Toxoplasma prophylaxis is for CD4 under 100 with positive IgG.

### usmle2-197 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Mild bleeding from ulcerative colitis limited to the rectum.
- Answer: A rectal medicine.
- Clue: Oral prednisone is not first.
- First-line: Topical mesalamine or steroid.
- Trap: Do not start a biologic.
- Source: ACG ulcerative colitis guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Mild ulcerative proctitis is treated topically.

### usmle2-198 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: A medium varix. The patient has never bled.
- Answer: A nonselective beta blocker.
- Clue: This is primary prevention.
- First-line: Start the blocker if pressure allows.
- Trap: Do not band every never-bled varix.
- Source: AASLD portal hypertension guidance (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Medium varices without bleeding get a nonselective beta blocker.

### usmle2-199 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Red-cell casts and a creatinine that doubled this week.
- Answer: Glomerulonephritis. Admit.
- Clue: Casts are glomerular, not bladder.
- First-line: Serologies and nephrology.
- Trap: Do not treat as cystitis.
- Source: KDIGO glomerulonephritis guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Red-cell casts with a rising creatinine are acute nephritis.

### usmle2-200 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: The worst headache, on warfarin, and blood in the cisterns.
- Answer: Reverse the warfarin.
- Clue: The bleed is subarachnoid.
- First-line: Four-factor concentrate and vitamin K.
- Trap: Do not give heparin.
- Source: AHA/ASA subarachnoid hemorrhage guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Anticoagulant-associated intracranial bleeding is reversed immediately.

### usmle2-201 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Orbital pain for 45 minutes with a tearing eye.
- Answer: Oxygen and an injected triptan.
- Clue: Oral pills are too slow.
- First-line: High-flow oxygen now.
- Trap: Do not use an opioid.
- Source: AHS cluster headache guidance (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Acute cluster headache is oxygen and subcutaneous sumatriptan.

### usmle2-202 · Musculoskeletal

- Outline: Musculoskeletal system
- Front: Night shoulder pain and a painful arc. Strength is intact.
- Answer: Physical therapy.
- Clue: A drop-arm test means a tear.
- First-line: Therapy first.
- Trap: Do not operate a strong shoulder.
- Source: AAOS rotator cuff guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Atraumatic cuff pain with preserved strength starts with therapy.

### usmle2-203 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: Platelets are 900,000 and iron is low. The patient feels well.
- Answer: Replace iron and recheck.
- Clue: Iron deficiency can raise platelets.
- First-line: Iron first.
- Trap: Do not start chemotherapy first.
- Source: NCCN myeloproliferative neoplasms (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Reactive thrombocytosis from iron deficiency is corrected before a marrow disease is labeled.

### usmle2-204 · Reproductive

- Outline: Pregnancy, childbirth, and the puerperium
- Front: Bleeding at 10 weeks and an open cervix.
- Answer: Inevitable pregnancy loss.
- Clue: A closed cervix with a heartbeat is a different diagnosis.
- First-line: Offer medication or aspiration.
- Trap: Do not send an open cervix home without a plan.
- Source: ACOG early pregnancy loss (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: An open cervix with bleeding is inevitable abortion.

### usmle2-205 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Infectious Diseases
- Front: A 12-month-old is due for vaccines.
- Answer: MMR and varicella start now.
- Clue: Live vaccines begin at this visit.
- First-line: Give the 12-month series.
- Trap: Do not delay for a mild cold.
- Source: CDC child immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: MMR and varicella are given at 12 months.

### usmle2-206 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: A mother with hepatitis B delivers.
- Answer: Vaccine and immunoglobulin within 12 hours.
- Clue: The vaccine alone is not enough.
- First-line: Give both.
- Trap: Do not wait for the clinic visit.
- Source: CDC perinatal hepatitis B (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: HBsAg-positive mothers: vaccine and HBIG within 12 hours.

### usmle2-207 · Emergency

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: Shock after a calcium-channel blocker overdose.
- Answer: Calcium and high-dose insulin.
- Clue: Fluids alone will not fix the heart.
- First-line: Call toxicology.
- Trap: Do not use one pressor as the only drug.
- Source: AHA cardiotoxic drug guidance (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Calcium-channel blocker shock is treated with calcium and high-dose insulin.

### usmle2-208 · Emergency

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A new left bundle-branch block and chest pain. No concordant ST elevation.
- Answer: A bundle branch alone is not a STEMI.
- Clue: Concordant ST elevation is the STEMI sign.
- First-line: Use the concordant rules.
- Trap: Do not thrombolyse a bundle branch alone.
- Source: AHA/ACC STEMI guideline (2013)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: New left bundle-branch block alone is not an automatic STEMI equivalent.

### usmle2-209 · Oncology

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: Back pain and leg weakness in a patient with cancer.
- Answer: Steroids and MRI now.
- Clue: This is cord compression until proven otherwise.
- First-line: Dexamethasone and urgent MRI.
- Trap: Do not wait for clinic.
- Source: NCCN spinal cord compression (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Suspected malignant cord compression gets immediate steroids and MRI.

### usmle2-210 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Cirrhosis, confusion, and asterixis.
- Answer: Lactulose.
- Clue: Find infection or bleeding, and still give the drug.
- First-line: Lactulose to two or three stools.
- Trap: Do not restrict protein as the treatment.
- Source: AASLD hepatic encephalopathy (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Lactulose is first-line for hepatic encephalopathy.

### usmle2-211 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Reproductive system
- Front: Syphilis in pregnancy. The allergy was a rash.
- Answer: Desensitize and give penicillin.
- Clue: Doxycycline is not used in pregnancy.
- First-line: Penicillin is the only drug.
- Trap: Do not substitute doxycycline.
- Source: CDC STI treatment guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Pregnant patients with syphilis receive penicillin, after desensitization if needed.

### usmle2-212 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: QRS is 160 ms with a left bundle pattern. Ejection fraction is low. Medicines are optimized.
- Answer: Refer for resynchronization.
- Clue: The wide QRS is the indication.
- First-line: Device referral.
- Trap: Do not add another diuretic instead.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: CRT is for LBBB, QRS at least 150 ms, and symptomatic HFrEF.

### usmle2-213 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Calcium is 10.8, PTH is high, and there is a kidney stone.
- Answer: Surgery.
- Clue: Stones are an indication.
- First-line: Refer for parathyroidectomy.
- Trap: Do not watch a stone former.
- Source: Endocrine Society primary hyperparathyroidism (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Kidney stones indicate parathyroidectomy.

### usmle2-214 · Renal

- Outline: Renal and urinary system
- Front: Potassium is 5.8 after an ACE inhibitor and a new NSAID.
- Answer: Stop the NSAID.
- Clue: The combination injures the kidney.
- First-line: Stop the NSAID and recheck.
- Trap: Do not add potassium.
- Source: KDIGO acute kidney injury guideline (2012)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: NSAIDs plus a RAS inhibitor cause hemodynamic AKI and hyperkalemia.

### usmle2-215 · Prevention

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: A BRCA1 carrier asks about ovarian cancer screening.
- Answer: Surgery prevents. Ultrasound does not.
- Clue: Discuss the timing of risk-reducing surgery.
- First-line: Refer for that counseling.
- Trap: Do not rely on an annual ultrasound.
- Source: NCCN hereditary breast and ovarian cancer (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Risk-reducing salpingo-oophorectomy is effective prevention. Screening ultrasound is not a substitute.

### usmle2-216 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: A 4-year-old has a fever and holds the hip flexed and turned out.
- Answer: Septic hip until excluded.
- Clue: Growing pains do not cause fever.
- First-line: Ultrasound and orthopedics.
- Trap: Do not call it a growing pain.
- Source: AAP musculoskeletal infection (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Fever plus a flexed externally rotated hip is septic arthritis until excluded.

### usmle2-217 · Emergency

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: Confusion and a carboxyhemoglobin of 25 percent after a fire.
- Answer: Oxygen, and discuss a pressure chamber.
- Clue: Confusion means severe poisoning.
- First-line: 100 percent oxygen now.
- Trap: Do not discharge after the headache fades.
- Source: Undersea and Hyperbaric Medical Society CO guidance (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Neurologic symptoms from carbon monoxide warrant hyperbaric consideration.

### usmle2-218 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: INR is 3.5 and surgery is in two hours.
- Answer: Four-factor concentrate and vitamin K.
- Clue: Vitamin K alone is too slow.
- First-line: Reverse, then operate.
- Trap: Do not wait a day.
- Source: ASH warfarin reversal guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Urgent surgery on warfarin is reversed with four-factor PCC and vitamin K.

### usmle2-219 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: Heartburn twice a week. Age 40. No trouble swallowing.
- Answer: An 8-week acid blocker.
- Clue: Alarms or age 60 mean a scope.
- First-line: Empiric therapy.
- Trap: Do not endoscope first.
- Source: ACG GERD guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Typical GERD without alarms gets an 8-week proton-pump inhibitor.

### usmle2-220 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: LDL is 70 on a maximal statin after a heart attack.
- Answer: Add ezetimibe.
- Clue: Very high risk aims under 70.
- First-line: Add ezetimibe.
- Trap: Do not stop the statin.
- Source: AHA/ACC cholesterol guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: LDL at or above 70 mg/dL on a maximal statin gets ezetimibe in very high risk.

### usmle2-221 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Pneumonia is improving. The patient asks how long the antibiotic lasts.
- Answer: Five days if stable and afebrile.
- Clue: Complications get longer courses.
- First-line: Stop at 5 days when criteria are met.
- Trap: Do not use 14 days by habit.
- Source: IDSA/ATS community-acquired pneumonia guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Five days is enough for uncomplicated CAP that has stabilized.

### usmle2-222 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: Myasthenia worsens after an aminoglycoside.
- Answer: Stop the drug.
- Clue: Several antibiotics worsen myasthenia.
- First-line: Support the airway and stop it.
- Trap: Do not add another aminoglycoside.
- Source: AAN myasthenia guidance (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Aminoglycosides can precipitate myasthenic crisis.

### usmle2-223 · Reproductive

- Outline: Pregnancy, childbirth, and the puerperium
- Front: An early scan showed a low placenta. She is 28 weeks and has not bled.
- Answer: Repeat the ultrasound.
- Clue: Many early previas resolve.
- First-line: Reimage.
- Trap: Do not book a cesarean from the first scan.
- Source: ACOG placenta previa guidance (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Early low placentas are reimaged because many resolve.

### usmle2-224 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: TSH is 12 on a stable levothyroxine dose. The patient takes calcium each morning.
- Answer: Separate calcium from the thyroid pill.
- Clue: Calcium blocks absorption.
- First-line: Separate by four hours, then recheck.
- Trap: Do not double the dose today.
- Source: ATA hypothyroidism guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Calcium and iron reduce levothyroxine absorption.

### usmle2-225 · Musculoskeletal

- Outline: Musculoskeletal system
- Front: Low back pain for two days. No red flags.
- Answer: Stay active. No MRI.
- Clue: Fever, cancer, deficit, or bladder symptoms are the flags.
- First-line: Activity and a nonopioid.
- Trap: Do not order an MRI.
- Source: ACP low back pain guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Acute low back pain without red flags is not imaged.

### usmle2-226 · Prevention

- Outline: Behavioral health
- Front: A 55-year-old has never been asked about tobacco here.
- Answer: Offer a drug plus counseling.
- Clue: Advice alone is weaker.
- First-line: Varenicline or nicotine replacement plus counseling.
- Trap: Do not stop at a pamphlet.
- Source: USPSTF tobacco cessation (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Pharmacotherapy plus counseling is the effective tobacco treatment.

### usmle2-227 · Hematologic

- Outline: Blood and lymphoreticular system
- Front: Hemoglobin is 8.2 and the patient has ischemic chest pain.
- Answer: Transfuse. The threshold is 8, not 7.
- Clue: The coronary exception is higher.
- First-line: Give blood.
- Trap: Do not apply the stable-patient rule of 7.
- Source: AABB transfusion guideline (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Acute coronary syndrome uses a threshold of 8 g/dL.

### usmle2-228 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: A TNF inhibitor is about to start. Tuberculosis has not been checked.
- Answer: Test first.
- Clue: The drug can reactivate tuberculosis.
- First-line: A release assay and a chest film.
- Trap: Do not start the biologic the same day.
- Source: ACG Crohn disease guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Latent TB is excluded before a TNF inhibitor.

### usmle2-229 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: Diabetes, stable angina, and an LDL of 110 on no statin.
- Answer: High-intensity statin.
- Clue: Vascular disease sets the intensity.
- First-line: Start it.
- Trap: Do not use diet alone.
- Source: AHA/ACC cholesterol guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Clinical ASCVD receives high-intensity statin therapy.

### usmle2-230 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Skin and subcutaneous tissue
- Front: A human bite to the hand is swollen at 12 hours.
- Answer: Antibiotics that cover mouth flora.
- Clue: Hand bites infect fast.
- First-line: Amoxicillin-clavulanate and a hand exam.
- Trap: Do not close it tightly.
- Source: IDSA skin and soft tissue guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Human bites of the hand need oral-flora coverage.

### usmle2-231 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: A 6-week-old has projectile nonbilious vomiting and an olive mass.
- Answer: Fix the electrolytes, then operate.
- Clue: Chloride is low and bicarbonate is high.
- First-line: Fluids first, then pyloromyotomy.
- Trap: Do not operate a dehydrated infant tonight.
- Source: AAP surgical principle (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Pyloric stenosis is repaired after chloride and bicarbonate are corrected.

### usmle2-232 · Emergency

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: Severe pressure after aged cheese on a monoamine oxidase inhibitor.
- Answer: An alpha blocker.
- Clue: The crisis is tyramine.
- First-line: Phentolamine.
- Trap: Do not lead with a beta blocker.
- Source: FDA MAOI interaction labeling (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Tyramine crisis is treated with phentolamine, not beta blockade alone.

### usmle2-233 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Home oxygen was started last month. The patient asks if it is forever.
- Answer: Recheck in a few months.
- Clue: Some patients no longer qualify.
- First-line: Repeat the saturation.
- Trap: Do not assume it is lifelong.
- Source: ATS home oxygen guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Oxygen qualification is reassessed after recovery.

### usmle2-234 · Reproductive

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: A simple 4 cm ovarian cyst at age 45. No symptoms.
- Answer: No follow-up imaging.
- Clue: Simple cysts 5 cm or smaller are normal before menopause.
- First-line: Reassure.
- Trap: Do not operate.
- Source: ACR ovarian cyst consensus (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Simple cysts 5 cm or smaller in premenopausal patients need no follow-up.

### usmle2-235 · Endocrine

- Outline: Endocrine system
- Front: Graves disease, not pregnant, wants definitive treatment, and is still very toxic.
- Answer: Calm the gland first.
- Clue: Iodine during a storm can worsen it.
- First-line: Make the patient euthyroid, then iodine or surgery.
- Trap: Do not give radioiodine during a storm.
- Source: ATA hyperthyroidism guideline (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Definitive therapy waits until severe thyrotoxicosis is controlled.

### usmle2-236 · GI

- Outline: Gastrointestinal system
- Front: A cirrhotic patient asks which pain drug is safest.
- Answer: Acetaminophen at a low dose. NSAIDs are worse.
- Clue: NSAIDs cause bleeding and kidney injury.
- First-line: Limit acetaminophen to 2 g a day.
- Trap: Do not prescribe ibuprofen.
- Source: AASLD cirrhosis guidance (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: NSAIDs are avoided in cirrhosis. Low-dose acetaminophen is preferred.

### usmle2-237 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A patient 2 weeks after a stent needs a tooth pulled.
- Answer: Do not stop both antiplatelet drugs.
- Clue: Aspirin stays. The second drug stays this early.
- First-line: Dental care on both drugs.
- Trap: Do not hold them for a cleaning.
- Source: ACC/AHA dual antiplatelet therapy (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Early after a stent, both antiplatelet drugs continue for dental work.

### usmle2-238 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Multisystem processes and disorders
- Front: Fever after returning from a falciparum region. The patient is confused.
- Answer: Intravenous artesunate.
- Clue: Oral chloroquine is the wrong drug.
- First-line: Artesunate now.
- Trap: Do not wait overnight for the smear.
- Source: CDC malaria treatment (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Severe malaria is intravenous artesunate.

### usmle2-239 · Prevention

- Outline: Musculoskeletal system
- Front: A 66-year-old woman has never had a bone-density test and has not broken a bone.
- Answer: Offer the density test.
- Clue: Sixty-five is the age.
- First-line: DEXA.
- Trap: Do not wait for a fracture.
- Source: USPSTF osteoporosis screening (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-2-ck-materials
- Why this source: Women 65 and older are screened for osteoporosis.

### usmle2-240 · Neurologic

- Outline: Nervous system and special senses
- Front: A seizure happened at sodium 118. Sodium is now 136.
- Answer: No chronic seizure drug.
- Clue: The provoke was the sodium.
- First-line: Counsel.
- Trap: Do not call it epilepsy tonight.
- Source: AAN first seizure guideline (2015)
