- Front: A 4-year-old is due for preschool vaccines.
- Answer: DTaP, polio, MMR, and varicella boosters.
- Clue: This is the 4- to 6-year visit.
- First-line: Give them.
- Trap: Do not restart the infant series.
- Source: CDC child immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Ages 4 to 6 get those boosters.

### usmle3-116 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: musculoskeletal system
- Front: One fall on a blood thinner. No injury.
- Answer: Do not stop the thinner.
- Clue: Stroke risk usually outweighs one fall.
- First-line: Find a reversible cause and continue.
- Trap: Do not stop the drug reflexively.
- Source: ACC/AHA atrial fibrillation guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A single fall is not an automatic reason to stop anticoagulation.

### usmle3-117 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: A family wants a sedative for nighttime wandering in dementia.
- Answer: Do not sedate.
- Clue: Those drugs increase falls and death.
- First-line: Daytime light and a calm evening.
- Trap: Do not use an antipsychotic as a sleeper.
- Source: AGS Beers Criteria (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Antipsychotics and benzodiazepines are avoided for insomnia in dementia.

### usmle3-118 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: Insomnia for 4 months. A handout did not help.
- Answer: Therapy for insomnia, not a nightly pill.
- Clue: CBT-I is first-line.
- First-line: Refer.
- Trap: Do not start a controlled drug.
- Source: ACP insomnia guideline (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: CBT-I is first-line for chronic insomnia.

### usmle3-119 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: perioperative care
- Front: Cataract surgery tomorrow. A beta blocker is a morning medicine.
- Answer: Take it.
- Clue: Stopping raises risk.
- First-line: Continue with a sip of water.
- Trap: Do not hold a chronic beta blocker.
- Source: ACC/AHA perioperative guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Chronic beta blockers are continued on the day of surgery.

### usmle3-120 · Endocrine

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: perioperative care
- Front: Metformin is scheduled the morning of a contrast CT.
- Answer: Hold it.
- Clue: Restart when eating and the kidney is stable.
- First-line: Hold the dose.
- Trap: Do not take it that morning.
- Source: FDA metformin labeling (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Metformin is held around iodinated contrast.

### usmle3-121 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Ascites and a normal sodium. The patient asks about fluids.
- Answer: Limit salt, not water.
- Clue: Fluid restriction is for a low sodium.
- First-line: About 2 g of sodium.
- Trap: Do not fluid-restrict a normal sodium.
- Source: AASLD ascites guidance (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Ascites is sodium restriction. Fluid restriction is for hyponatremia.

### usmle3-122 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Tense ascites and shortness of breath.
- Answer: Drain it.
- Clue: A large volume needs albumin.
- First-line: Paracentesis.
- Trap: Do not only raise the diuretic.
- Source: AASLD ascites guidance (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Tense ascites is drained. Albumin is given above 5 liters.

### usmle3-123 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Weight is up 2 kg in 3 days. The patient can still lie flat.
- Answer: Raise the diuretic for a few days.
- Clue: Daily weights catch congestion early.
- First-line: A short increase.
- Trap: Do not wait 6 weeks.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A gain of about 2 kg is a reason to adjust the diuretic.

### usmle3-124 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: Age 45 and never screened for colon cancer.
- Answer: Start now.
- Clue: Forty-five is the age.
- First-line: Stool test or colonoscopy.
- Trap: Do not wait for 50.
- Source: USPSTF colorectal cancer screening (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Screening starts at age 45.

### usmle3-125 · Prevention

- Outline: Respiratory system
- Front: Age 55, a pack a day for 20 years, still smoking.
- Answer: Annual low-dose CT.
- Clue: Age 50 to 80 and 20 pack-years qualify.
- First-line: Order the CT.
- Trap: Do not order a chest x-ray as the screen.
- Source: USPSTF lung cancer screening (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Low-dose CT is the screening test. A chest x-ray is not.

### usmle3-126 · Prevention

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: Age 42 and never a mammogram. Average risk.
- Answer: Biennial mammography.
- Clue: The range is 40 to 74.
- First-line: Every other year.
- Trap: Do not wait until 50.
- Source: USPSTF breast cancer screening (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Biennial mammography runs from 40 to 74.

### usmle3-127 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: Hives from eggs and the flu shot is due.
- Answer: Give the vaccine.
- Clue: Egg allergy is not a contraindication.
- First-line: Any age-appropriate flu vaccine.
- Trap: Do not send them away.
- Source: CDC influenza vaccine guidance (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Egg allergy does not forbid influenza vaccine.

### usmle3-128 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: Shingles 5 years ago. Age 58.
- Answer: Still give the recombinant series.
- Clue: Past shingles does not replace the vaccine.
- First-line: Two doses.
- Trap: Do not skip it.
- Source: CDC adult immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Recombinant zoster vaccine is given at 50 and older even after shingles.

### usmle3-129 · Behavioral

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: medication use
- Front: Day 3 after surgery. Walking. Still on an opioid.
- Answer: Stop the opioid.
- Clue: Acute pain is a few days.
- First-line: Switch to a nonopioid.
- Trap: Do not refill 30 days.
- Source: CDC opioid guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Postoperative opioids are days, not a month.

### usmle3-130 · Behavioral

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: medication use
- Front: Chronic pain and a request to raise a long-acting opioid.
- Answer: Do not escalate as the plan.
- Clue: Function is the goal.
- First-line: Offer nonopioid care.
- Trap: Do not raise the dose by habit.
- Source: CDC opioid guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Dose escalation is not the primary response in chronic pain.

### usmle3-131 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A year of amiodarone and no TSH ever.
- Answer: Check it.
- Clue: The drug pushes the thyroid either way.
- First-line: TSH now.
- Trap: Do not wait for a tremor.
- Source: ATA amiodarone guidance (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: TSH is checked before and during amiodarone.

### usmle3-132 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Age 80, eGFR 30, and a new digoxin plan.
- Answer: Use a low dose.
- Clue: The kidney clears it.
- First-line: A low dose and a level.
- Trap: Do not use a young-adult dose.
- Source: AHA digoxin guidance (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Digoxin is renally cleared and dosed down in CKD.

### usmle3-133 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Lithium, and a new thiazide today.
- Answer: Check the lithium level.
- Clue: Thiazides raise lithium.
- First-line: Recheck within days.
- Trap: Do not wait for confusion.
- Source: APA lithium monitoring (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A new thiazide is a reason to recheck the lithium level.

### usmle3-134 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: Age 76 and a request to start aspirin to prevent a first heart attack.
- Answer: Do not start it.
- Clue: Bleeding outweighs the gain.
- First-line: Talk about pressure and a statin if indicated.
- Trap: Do not start primary-prevention aspirin.
- Source: USPSTF aspirin for primary prevention (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Starting aspirin for primary prevention is not recommended at 60 and older.

### usmle3-135 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A stent last year and a request to stop aspirin because they feel fine.
- Answer: Aspirin continues.
- Clue: The second drug may stop. Aspirin does not.
- First-line: Continue aspirin.
- Trap: Do not stop both.
- Source: ACC/AHA dual antiplatelet therapy (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Aspirin continues indefinitely after a stent.

### usmle3-136 · Ethics

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: communication
- Front: A capable patient refuses a transfusion.
- Answer: Honor it.
- Clue: Document what they understood.
- First-line: Offer alternatives.
- Trap: Do not transfuse because you disagree.
- Source: AMA Code of Medical Ethics on refusal (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A capable refusal is honored.

### usmle3-137 · Behavioral

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: A daily heavy drinker is admitted. No tremor yet.
- Answer: Start a withdrawal scale now.
- Clue: A seizure is late.
- First-line: Monitor from admission.
- Trap: Do not wait for the shake.
- Source: ASAM alcohol withdrawal guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Daily drinkers are monitored for withdrawal from admission.

### usmle3-138 · Prevention

- Outline: Cardiovascular system
- Front: A 70-year-old woman who smoked asks for an aneurysm screen.
- Answer: Routine screening is for men.
- Clue: The tested group is men 65 to 75 who smoked.
- First-line: Explain that.
- Trap: Do not ultrasound her yearly.
- Source: USPSTF abdominal aortic aneurysm screening (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Routine screening is not for women who smoked.

### usmle3-139 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: A college student has never had a meningococcal vaccine.
- Answer: Give it.
- Clue: Dorm living is a reason to be current.
- First-line: Vaccinate.
- Trap: Do not wait for an outbreak.
- Source: CDC meningococcal vaccine (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Young adults need meningococcal conjugate vaccine.

### usmle3-140 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: infectious disease
- Front: An 11-year-old is due for a tetanus booster.
- Answer: Tdap.
- Clue: Infant DTaP is the wrong product.
- First-line: Give Tdap.
- Trap: Do not give DTaP.
- Source: CDC child immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: The 11- to 12-year booster is Tdap.

### usmle3-141 · Reproductive

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: female reproductive system
- Front: Migraine without aura, normal pressure, and a request for a combined pill.
- Answer: A combined pill is acceptable.
- Clue: Aura is the contraindication.
- First-line: Offer it if she wants it.
- Trap: Do not refuse estrogen for migraine without aura.
- Source: CDC Medical Eligibility Criteria (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Migraine without aura can use combined contraception.

### usmle3-142 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Celiac disease, a year of a strict diet, and the patient feels well.
- Answer: Stay gluten free.
- Clue: Feeling well is not a cure.
- First-line: Continue the diet.
- Trap: Do not rechallenge.
- Source: ACG celiac guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Celiac disease is a lifelong gluten-free diet.

### usmle3-143 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Adrenal insufficiency and vomiting at home.
- Answer: Stress-dose and seek care.
- Clue: Pills may not stay down.
- First-line: Triple the steroid and urgent care.
- Trap: Do not wait for tomorrow.
- Source: Endocrine Society adrenal insufficiency (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Vomiting on steroid replacement is an emergency.

### usmle3-144 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Years of prednisone and a plan to stop tomorrow.
- Answer: Taper.
- Clue: The adrenal may not wake up.
- First-line: A taper.
- Trap: Do not stop abruptly.
- Source: Endocrine Society adrenal insufficiency (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Long-term steroids are tapered.

### usmle3-145 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Angina only at hard work, a normal squeeze, and good medicines.
- Answer: Medicines, not a stent first.
- Clue: A stent is not better for this symptom.
- First-line: Continue medical therapy.
- Trap: Do not refer for a stent first.
- Source: ACC chronic coronary disease guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Stable symptoms on therapy are not a stent-first disease.

### usmle3-146 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Pregnant and well-controlled asthma.
- Answer: Continue the inhaled steroid.
- Clue: Uncontrolled asthma is the greater risk.
- First-line: Continue.
- Trap: Do not stop the controller.
- Source: GINA strategy (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Inhaled corticosteroids continue in pregnancy.

### usmle3-147 · Pediatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A breastfed newborn is not back to birth weight at 14 days.
- Answer: Same-day feeding help.
- Clue: Most are back by 14 days.
- First-line: Lactation support today.
- Trap: Do not wait another week.
- Source: AAP breastfeeding guidance (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Failure to regain birth weight by 14 days needs a feeding assessment.

### usmle3-148 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A fall and a 30-point drop in pressure on standing.
- Answer: Treat the drop.
- Clue: The sitting number can look fine.
- First-line: Reduce the blood-pressure drugs.
- Trap: Do not add another drug.
- Source: ACC/AHA high blood pressure guideline (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Orthostatic hypotension means the regimen is too intensive.

### usmle3-149 · Neurologic

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: nervous system and special senses
- Front: Years without a seizure and a wish to stop the drug.
- Answer: Discuss a taper. Do not stop tomorrow.
- Clue: Relapse is the risk.
- First-line: A shared taper plan.
- Trap: Do not stop abruptly.
- Source: AAN antiseizure withdrawal (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Withdrawal is individualized and tapered after a long seizure-free period.

### usmle3-150 · Dermatologic

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: skin
- Front: A changing mole offered for freezing.
- Answer: Excise it.
- Clue: Freezing destroys the depth.
- First-line: Excisional biopsy.
- Trap: Do not freeze it.
- Source: AAD melanoma guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Suspected melanoma is excised, not destroyed.

### usmle3-151 · Hematologic

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: hematologic system
- Front: INR has been 2.5 for months.
- Answer: Recheck in up to 12 weeks.
- Clue: Weekly checks are for unstable values.
- First-line: Extend the interval.
- Trap: Do not check every week.
- Source: ACC warfarin management (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Stable INRs can be checked every 12 weeks.

### usmle3-152 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Cirrhosis and a MELD that rose from 12 to 22.
- Answer: Refer for transplant evaluation.
- Clue: Do not wait for intensive care.
- First-line: Refer.
- Trap: Do not wait for the first bleed.
- Source: AASLD transplant referral (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A rising MELD prompts transplant evaluation.

### usmle3-153 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Type 1 diabetes in pregnancy and an A1c goal written under 8.
- Answer: Tighten carefully.
- Clue: Pregnancy goals are lower if lows can be avoided.
- First-line: Aim nearer 6 to 6.5.
- Trap: Do not use the older-adult goal.
- Source: ADA diabetes in pregnancy (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Pregnancy goals are tighter than goals for complex older adults.

### usmle3-154 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: PCOS, not trying to conceive, and no periods.
- Answer: Protect the lining.
- Clue: Unopposed estrogen raises cancer risk.
- First-line: A hormonal method.
- Trap: Do not leave her without a bleed plan.
- Source: Endocrine Society PCOS guideline (2013)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Amenorrhea in PCOS needs endometrial protection.

### usmle3-155 · Reproductive

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: female reproductive system
- Front: Hot flashes, age 49, and a uterus.
- Answer: Estrogen plus a progestin.
- Clue: Estrogen alone is for no uterus.
- First-line: Combined therapy.
- Trap: Do not give estrogen alone.
- Source: NAMS hormone therapy position (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A uterus requires a progestin with systemic estrogen.

### usmle3-156 · Reproductive

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: female reproductive system
- Front: Hot flashes and no uterus.
- Answer: Estrogen alone.
- Clue: A progestin is not needed.
- First-line: Estrogen.
- Trap: Do not add a progestin by habit.
- Source: NAMS hormone therapy position (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: After hysterectomy, estrogen is used alone.

### usmle3-157 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: A 30-year-old man who has sex with men has not had an HIV test this year.
- Answer: Test at least yearly.
- Clue: Higher risk is more than once in a lifetime.
- First-line: Offer the test today.
- Trap: Do not wait for symptoms.
- Source: USPSTF HIV screening (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Increased risk means at least annual HIV testing.

### usmle3-158 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: HIV treatment started last month.
- Answer: Check the viral load in about 4 weeks.
- Clue: Failure is a load that does not fall.
- First-line: Check at a month.
- Trap: Do not wait a year.
- Source: DHHS antiretroviral guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Viral load is checked about 4 weeks after a start.

### usmle3-159 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Calcium stones and low fluid intake.
- Answer: Two liters of urine a day.
- Clue: Water is the first drug.
- First-line: Increase fluids.
- Trap: Do not start allopurinol for a calcium stone.
- Source: AUA kidney stone medical management (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: High urine volume is first-line stone prevention.

### usmle3-160 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Uric acid stones and a urine pH of 5.
- Answer: Alkalinize.
- Clue: Citrate raises the pH.
- First-line: Potassium citrate.
- Trap: Do not use a calcium binder as the plan.
- Source: AUA kidney stone medical management (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Uric acid stones are treated by raising urine pH.

### usmle3-161 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Euvolemic heart failure and a climbing creatinine on a high diuretic dose.
- Answer: Lower the water pill.
- Clue: The disease drugs stay.
- First-line: Reduce the loop.
- Trap: Do not stop the beta blocker.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Diuretics follow volume. Disease drugs are not stopped for a dry patient.

### usmle3-162 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Uncomplicated flu at home.
- Answer: Stay home until fever-free for 24 hours.
- Clue: The drug shortens symptoms. It does not replace staying home.
- First-line: Stay home a day after the fever.
- Trap: Do not return to work the hour it breaks.
- Source: CDC influenza isolation (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Stay home until fever-free for 24 hours without fever medicine.

### usmle3-163 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Left-lower pain, no fever, and known diverticulosis. The belly is soft.
- Answer: Home care is reasonable.
- Clue: Fever or inability to drink means admission.
- First-line: Follow closely.
- Trap: Do not give intravenous drugs for a soft exam.
- Source: AGA diverticulitis guideline (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Mild uncomplicated diverticulitis can be treated at home.

### usmle3-164 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: CT shows a 3 cm diverticular abscess.
- Answer: Drain it in this setting.
- Clue: Small inflammation can be medical. This cannot.
- First-line: Drainage and antibiotics.
- Trap: Do not send a 3 cm abscess home.
- Source: ASCRS diverticulitis guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Abscesses about 3 cm and larger are drained.

### usmle3-165 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: Buprenorphine for opioid use disorder, admitted for pneumonia.
- Answer: Continue it.
- Clue: Stopping precipitates withdrawal.
- First-line: Continue the dose.
- Trap: Do not hold it because it is controlled.
- Source: ASAM opioid use disorder guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Buprenorphine is continued in the hospital.

### usmle3-166 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: A request to stop buprenorphine after 2 stable months.
- Answer: Longer is better.
- Clue: Months beat weeks.
- First-line: Continue.
- Trap: Do not taper at the first stable month.
- Source: ASAM opioid use disorder guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Longer treatment has better outcomes than short tapers.

### usmle3-167 · Pharmacology

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: medication use
- Front: Omeprazole and clopidogrel together.
- Answer: Do not stop the antiplatelet drug.
- Clue: A different acid drug can be used.
- First-line: Prefer pantoprazole if both are needed.
- Trap: Do not stop clopidogrel.
- Source: FDA clopidogrel interaction (2010)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Omeprazole may reduce clopidogrel activation. The antiplatelet drug stays.

### usmle3-168 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Latent tuberculosis and pregnancy. The chest film is normal.
- Answer: Treat with a pregnancy-suitable regimen.
- Clue: Active disease is never delayed. Latent disease is treated with care.
- First-line: Add pyridoxine.
- Trap: Do not use pyrazinamide casually.
- Source: CDC tuberculosis in pregnancy (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Latent TB in pregnancy uses a suitable regimen plus pyridoxine.

### usmle3-169 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A man under 65 with atrial fibrillation and no other stroke risks.
- Answer: No anticoagulation.
- Clue: A score of zero is low risk.
- First-line: Do not anticoagulate.
- Trap: Do not use aspirin as the stroke drug.
- Source: ACC/AHA atrial fibrillation guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Low-risk atrial fibrillation is not anticoagulated.

### usmle3-170 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Atrial fibrillation, age, and hypertension. The score is elevated from those, not from sex alone.
- Answer: Anticoagulate.
- Clue: Sex alone does not decide.
- First-line: A direct oral anticoagulant.
- Trap: Do not substitute aspirin.
- Source: ACC/AHA atrial fibrillation guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Elevated stroke risk gets anticoagulation. Sex alone is not the reason.

### usmle3-171 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Home oxygen at 2 liters and a saturation of 99 percent.
- Answer: Turn it down.
- Clue: The target is not 99 percent.
- First-line: Reduce the flow.
- Trap: Do not leave them over-oxygenated.
- Source: BTS oxygen guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Oxygen is titrated to a target. 99 percent means the flow is high.

### usmle3-172 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: skin
- Front: A drained abscess is healed. The patient is well.
- Answer: Stop antibiotics if the drainage was complete.
- Clue: Drainage was the treatment.
- First-line: No long course.
- Trap: Do not use 14 days after a simple drain.
- Source: IDSA skin and soft tissue guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: An uncomplicated drained abscess may not need antibiotics.

### usmle3-173 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: A kidney transplant and a request for ibuprofen.
- Answer: Acetaminophen.
- Clue: NSAIDs threaten the graft.
- First-line: Acetaminophen.
- Trap: Do not use ibuprofen.
- Source: KDIGO transplant recipient guideline (2009)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: NSAIDs are avoided after kidney transplant.

### usmle3-174 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Dizzy and 118 over 70 on three drugs.
- Answer: Reduce a drug.
- Clue: Below goal with symptoms is too much medicine.
- First-line: Stop or lower one.
- Trap: Do not add a fourth.
- Source: ACC/AHA high blood pressure guideline (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Symptoms below goal mean the regimen is too intensive.

### usmle3-175 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: Age 78, poor health, and a first colonoscopy request.
- Answer: Do not start if life is short.
- Clue: After 75, screening is selective.
- First-line: Discuss and usually do not start.
- Trap: Do not schedule by age alone.
- Source: USPSTF colorectal cancer screening (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Starting screening late in poor health is not useful.

### usmle3-176 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Iron deficiency continues on a strict gluten-free diet.
- Answer: Recheck the diet and the serology.
- Clue: The diet may not be strict.
- First-line: Review and repeat labs.
- Trap: Do not ignore a falling hemoglobin.
- Source: ACG celiac guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Persistent iron deficiency on treatment needs reassessment.

### usmle3-177 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: An SGLT2 inhibitor is being started at an eGFR of 25.
- Answer: Check the floor. Many cannot be started this low.
- Clue: Labels differ. Starting below the floor is wrong.
- First-line: Do not start below the threshold.
- Trap: Do not assume every drug is kidney-safe.
- Source: ADA and KDIGO diabetes in CKD (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: SGLT2 inhibitors have eGFR floors for initiation.

### usmle3-178 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A patient with HFrEF is euvolemic and asks if the loop diuretic can be the drug they never change.
- Answer: The diuretic follows the weight. The other drugs do not.
- Clue: Volume drugs move. Disease drugs stay.
- First-line: Adjust the loop to weight.
- Trap: Do not freeze a high diuretic dose.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Loop diuretics are titrated to edema. Disease-modifying drugs are scheduled.

### usmle3-179 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A patient on insulin has a morning glucose of 200 and a 3 a.m. glucose of 70.
- Answer: The night dose is too high.
- Clue: The dawn number is a rebound from a low.
- First-line: Reduce the evening basal.
- Trap: Do not raise the morning dose.
- Source: ADA insulin titration (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A low 3 a.m. glucose with a high morning glucose means too much night insulin.

### usmle3-180 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: A 64-year-old woman at average risk had a normal mammogram this year.
- Answer: The next one is in 2 years.
- Clue: Biennial is the interval.
- First-line: Two years.
- Trap: Do not order it annually by habit.
- Source: USPSTF breast cancer screening (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Average-risk mammography is every other year.

### usmle3-181 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: A patient with HIV has an undetectable viral load and asks if they can transmit sexually.
- Answer: With a sustained undetectable load, sexual transmission is not observed.
- Clue: This is U equals U.
- First-line: Keep taking the drugs.
- Trap: Do not stop condoms advice for other infections.
- Source: DHHS antiretroviral guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A sustained undetectable viral load prevents sexual transmission of HIV.

### usmle3-182 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: A patient starting long-term prednisone asks about the stomach.
- Answer: A PPI is reasonable if other bleed risks exist.
- Clue: Steroids alone are a weaker indication than steroids plus an NSAID.
- First-line: Add protection if an NSAID or a prior bleed is present.
- Trap: Do not start a PPI for every short steroid burst.
- Source: AGA PPI de-prescribing (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: PPI prophylaxis is for higher bleed risk, not every steroid.

### usmle3-183 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Potassium is 5.2 and the patient feels well on an ACE inhibitor for protein in the urine.
- Answer: Recheck. Do not stop the ACE for 5.2.
- Clue: A modest rise can be diet.
- First-line: Review salt substitutes and recheck.
- Trap: Do not stop the kidney drug for 5.2 alone.
- Source: KDIGO chronic kidney disease guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Mild hyperkalemia is managed. The RAS inhibitor is not automatically stopped.

### usmle3-184 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A patient 10 days after a stent wants both blood thinners held for a cleaning.
- Answer: Do not hold them.
- Clue: The stent is young.
- First-line: Dental care on both drugs.
- Trap: Do not stop dual therapy in week two.
- Source: ACC/AHA dual antiplatelet therapy (2016)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Early after stenting, dental work happens on both antiplatelet drugs.

### usmle3-185 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Oxygen saturation is 93 percent at rest in a patient with COPD who is not short of breath.
- Answer: No home oxygen.
- Clue: The qualifying rest number is 88 percent or below.
- First-line: Do not prescribe oxygen.
- Trap: Do not treat 93 percent.
- Source: GOLD report (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Long-term oxygen is for resting saturation at or below 88 percent.

### usmle3-186 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: A patient on methadone is admitted for surgery.
- Answer: Continue methadone.
- Clue: Holding it causes withdrawal and pain.
- First-line: Confirm the dose and continue.
- Trap: Do not replace it with a short-acting opioid alone.
- Source: ASAM opioid use disorder guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Methadone for opioid use disorder is continued in the hospital.

### usmle3-187 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A patient with type 2 diabetes and an A1c of 6.4 asks to stop metformin because they feel fine.
- Answer: Continue if tolerated.
- Clue: The drug is doing the work.
- First-line: Keep it.
- Trap: Do not stop at the first normal A1c.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Metformin is continued when it is tolerated and the A1c is at goal.

### usmle3-188 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: A patient with cirrhosis and sodium of 128 is put on a free-water limit and still drinks 3 liters.
- Answer: Limit free water.
- Clue: Salt is already limited. Water is the extra step when sodium is low.
- First-line: Restrict free water.
- Trap: Do not give normal saline to raise a dilutional sodium.
- Source: AASLD ascites guidance (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Hypervolemic hyponatremia in cirrhosis is treated with free-water restriction.

### usmle3-189 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: A 50-year-old has never received a pneumococcal conjugate vaccine.
- Answer: Give it in this setting.
- Clue: Fifty is the current adult age.
- First-line: One conjugate vaccine.
- Trap: Do not wait for 65.
- Source: CDC adult immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Adults 50 and older are recommended a pneumococcal conjugate vaccine.

### usmle3-190 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Home readings stay above goal on two drugs at half doses.
- Answer: Raise both toward full doses before a third.
- Clue: Underdosing two drugs is common.
- First-line: Titrate, then add.
- Trap: Do not add a third drug at baby doses.
- Source: ACC/AHA high blood pressure guideline (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Combination drugs are titrated before a third agent.

### usmle3-191 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A patient on basal-bolus insulin corrects every high sugar and never changes the meal dose.
- Answer: Fix the meal dose. A correction is a patch.
- Clue: Repeated corrections mean the standing dose is wrong.
- First-line: Adjust the mealtime dose.
- Trap: Do not stack corrections.
- Source: ADA insulin titration (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Pattern corrections mean the scheduled dose should change.

### usmle3-192 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: A patient with cirrhosis and a sodium of 122 is told to drink sports drinks.
- Answer: Stop the free water.
- Clue: Sports drinks are free water here.
- First-line: Restrict free water.
- Trap: Do not give hypotonic fluid.
- Source: AASLD ascites guidance (2021)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Hypotonic fluid worsens hypervolemic hyponatremia in cirrhosis.

### usmle3-193 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: A patient on HIV treatment has a viral load of 800 after being undetectable.
- Answer: Check adherence before you change drugs.
- Clue: A blip or a miss is more common than resistance.
- First-line: Review doses, then repeat the load.
- Trap: Do not switch regimens the same day.
- Source: DHHS antiretroviral guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A newly detectable load is an adherence problem until a repeat confirms failure.

### usmle3-194 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: A patient with COPD is prescribed chronic azithromycin for frequent exacerbations and has a long QT.
- Answer: Do not use the macrolide.
- Clue: The benefit is for former smokers with frequent flares, and QT is a stop.
- First-line: Choose another path.
- Trap: Do not ignore the QT.
- Source: GOLD report (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Azithromycin for exacerbation prevention is avoided when the QT is long.

### usmle3-195 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A patient with HFrEF and a potassium of 4.8 is about to start spironolactone.
- Answer: Start it and recheck in a week.
- Clue: The drug is indicated. The potassium is acceptable to begin.
- First-line: Start and check labs soon.
- Trap: Do not wait for a potassium of 4.0.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Spironolactone is started when potassium is in a safe range, with an early recheck.

### usmle3-196 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: A patient with a kidney stone was told to drink a glass of water a day.
- Answer: Aim for at least 2.5 liters of urine.
- Clue: A glass is not enough.
- First-line: Set a urine-volume goal.
- Trap: Do not restrict calcium in the diet.
- Source: AUA kidney stone medical management (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Stone formers target high urine volume. Dietary calcium is not restricted.

### usmle3-197 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: A 26-year-old woman had a normal Pap last year and wants one today.
- Answer: She is not due.
- Clue: The interval is 3 years.
- First-line: Schedule it in 2 years.
- Trap: Do not screen annually.
- Source: USPSTF cervical cancer screening (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Cytology every 3 years means a normal test is not repeated yearly.

### usmle3-198 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: A patient on sertraline for 2 weeks says it is not working.
- Answer: Wait until 4 to 6 weeks before calling it a failure.
- Clue: Early side effects are not failure.
- First-line: Continue and recheck.
- Trap: Do not switch at week two.
- Source: APA depression guideline (2010)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Antidepressant trials need several weeks before a switch.

### usmle3-199 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: An 84-year-old with limited meals has a sliding-scale insulin order and no basal insulin.
- Answer: Add basal. A sliding scale alone fails.
- Clue: Sliding scale treats yesterday's sugar.
- First-line: Scheduled basal plus mealtime if eating.
- Trap: Do not use a sliding scale as sole therapy.
- Source: ADA hospital diabetes care (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Sliding-scale insulin alone is not recommended.

### usmle3-200 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: perioperative care
- Front: A patient needs a tooth extraction 3 months after a drug-eluting stent for stable angina.
- Answer: Delay elective surgery if the second drug cannot be stopped safely.
- Clue: Three months is early.
- First-line: Talk with cardiology before any hold.
- Trap: Do not stop both drugs.
- Source: ACC/AHA perioperative guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Elective surgery is delayed until the minimum dual-therapy window has passed.

### usmle3-201 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: A patient with influenza is day 3, worsening, and pregnant.
- Answer: Start oseltamivir even though day 1 has passed.
- Clue: Pregnancy is a reason to treat beyond the ideal window.
- First-line: Start the antiviral.
- Trap: Do not withhold it because 48 hours passed.
- Source: CDC influenza treatment (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Pregnant patients are treated with oseltamivir even after 48 hours if they are ill.

### usmle3-202 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: A patient with ulcerative colitis in remission on mesalamine wants to stop because they feel well.
- Answer: Continue maintenance.
- Clue: Stopping raises the relapse risk.
- First-line: Keep the mesalamine.
- Trap: Do not stop the maintainer.
- Source: ACG ulcerative colitis guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Mesalamine maintenance continues in remission.

### usmle3-203 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A patient with hypothyroidism takes the pill at bedtime with a snack and a TSH is high.
- Answer: Move it to an empty stomach.
- Clue: Food cuts absorption.
- First-line: Morning or a true empty stomach.
- Trap: Do not keep raising the dose around food.
- Source: ATA hypothyroidism guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Consistent empty-stomach dosing comes before dose escalation.

### usmle3-204 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: A patient on oxygen at 4 liters has a headache and a high morning carbon dioxide.
- Answer: Too much oxygen.
- Clue: Retainers are titrated to 88 to 92 percent.
- First-line: Turn the flow down.
- Trap: Do not chase 98 percent.
- Source: BTS oxygen guideline (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Hypercapnic patients are targeted to 88 to 92 percent saturation.

### usmle3-205 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Heart rate is 96 in HFrEF and an old asthma label was never wheezing.
- Answer: Start an evidence-based beta blocker.
- Clue: A remote label is not a current ban.
- First-line: Use carvedilol, metoprolol succinate, or bisoprolol.
- Trap: Do not use atenolol for heart failure.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: HFrEF uses one of three evidence-based beta blockers.

### usmle3-206 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Every high sugar gets a correction and the meal dose never changes.
- Answer: Change the meal dose.
- Clue: Repeated corrections mean the standing dose is wrong.
- First-line: Adjust the scheduled dose.
- Trap: Do not stack corrections.
- Source: ADA insulin titration (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Pattern corrections mean the scheduled insulin should change.

### usmle3-207 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Hepatitis C treatment ended 12 weeks ago and the viral load was not repeated.
- Answer: Check for cure.
- Clue: A negative RNA at 12 weeks is the cure test.
- First-line: Order the RNA.
- Trap: Do not assume cure.
- Source: AASLD/IDSA hepatitis C guidance (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Sustained response is checked 12 weeks after treatment.

### usmle3-208 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: CD4 is 50, toxoplasma IgG is positive, and HIV treatment starts today.
- Answer: Start treatment and prophylaxis together.
- Clue: Prophylaxis does not delay HIV treatment.
- First-line: Start both.
- Trap: Do not wait to treat HIV.
- Source: DHHS antiretroviral guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: ART is not delayed while prophylaxis is arranged.

### usmle3-209 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: The asthma inhaler is a dry powder and the breath is weak.
- Answer: Change the device.
- Clue: A dry powder fails without a strong breath.
- First-line: Use a mist or a spacer.
- Trap: Do not keep the same device.
- Source: GINA strategy (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Device choice must match inspiratory strength.

### usmle3-210 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Gout and stage 4 CKD. Allopurinol is planned at 300 mg tomorrow.
- Answer: Start allopurinol low.
- Clue: The kidney clears it.
- First-line: Begin low and titrate.
- Trap: Do not start at 300 mg.
- Source: ACR gout guideline (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Allopurinol starts low in CKD.

### usmle3-211 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: Diabetes at age 36 and lipids have never been checked.
- Answer: Check lipids now.
- Clue: Do not wait for age 40.
- First-line: Order a lipid panel.
- Trap: Do not defer the test.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Adults with diabetes have lipids assessed.

### usmle3-212 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: New suicidal thoughts one week after an antidepressant starts.
- Answer: See them this week.
- Clue: Early worsening needs contact.
- First-line: Same-week visit.
- Trap: Do not refill and wait a month.
- Source: FDA antidepressant monitoring (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Mood worsening soon after a start needs prompt review.

### usmle3-213 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Falls and a long-acting sulfonylurea.
- Answer: Stop that sulfonylurea.
- Clue: Glyburide causes prolonged lows.
- First-line: Use a safer drug.
- Trap: Do not continue glyburide.
- Source: AGS Beers Criteria (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Long-acting sulfonylureas are avoided in older adults.

### usmle3-214 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: perioperative care
- Front: A hip fracture and a direct oral anticoagulant this morning.
- Answer: Wait the drug-specific interval, not a month.
- Clue: The repair should not be cancelled for weeks.
- First-line: Time the operation.
- Trap: Do not operate the same afternoon.
- Source: ACC periprocedural anticoagulation (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Fracture surgery waits the anticoagulant interval, not weeks.

### usmle3-215 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: A pregnant household contact of influenza is still well on day 1.
- Answer: Offer prophylaxis.
- Clue: Pregnancy is a high-risk exposure.
- First-line: Start oseltamivir prophylaxis.
- Trap: Do not wait for fever.
- Source: CDC influenza antiviral guidance (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: High-risk contacts can receive post-exposure prophylaxis.

### usmle3-216 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Ulcerative colitis has been quiet on mesalamine for 2 years.
- Answer: Keep the mesalamine.
- Clue: Quiet years are not a stop signal.
- First-line: Continue maintenance.
- Trap: Do not trial off without a plan.
- Source: ACG ulcerative colitis guideline (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Maintenance continues through remission.

### usmle3-217 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Levothyroxine is taken with fiber cereal and the TSH is high.
- Answer: Separate it from fiber.
- Clue: Fiber cuts absorption.
- First-line: Empty-stomach dosing, then recheck.
- Trap: Do not add a second thyroid drug.
- Source: ATA hypothyroidism guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Food and fiber reduce levothyroxine absorption.

### usmle3-218 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Asthma is worse and nobody has watched an inhalation.
- Answer: Watch a puff before adding a drug.
- Clue: Bad technique looks like drug failure.
- First-line: Coach the device.
- Trap: Do not add a biologic first.
- Source: GINA strategy (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Technique is checked before therapy is escalated.

### usmle3-219 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Calcium stones and a low-dairy diet.
- Answer: Do not cut dietary calcium.
- Clue: Low calcium intake can raise urine oxalate.
- First-line: Keep normal dietary calcium.
- Trap: Do not prescribe a low-calcium diet.
- Source: AUA kidney stone medical management (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Dietary calcium is maintained in calcium stone formers.

### usmle3-220 · Prevention

- Outline: Female reproductive system and breast
- Front: A negative HPV test was 4 years ago. She is 66.
- Answer: She is due in a year.
- Clue: Primary HPV testing is every 5 years.
- First-line: Return next year.
- Trap: Do not repeat it today.
- Source: USPSTF cervical cancer screening (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Primary HPV screening is every 5 years.

### usmle3-221 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: Bupropion and a request to add an MAOI.
- Answer: Do not combine them.
- Clue: The washout is 14 days.
- First-line: Stop one and wait.
- Trap: Do not overlap the drugs.
- Source: FDA bupropion labeling (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Bupropion and MAOIs need a 14-day gap.

### usmle3-222 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Carvedilol and a heart rate of 50 with fatigue.
- Answer: Reduce the beta blocker one step.
- Clue: Symptomatic bradycardia means the dose is too high.
- First-line: Reduce it.
- Trap: Do not stop it abruptly.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Symptomatic bradycardia is a reason to reduce, not abruptly stop, the blocker.

### usmle3-223 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: Mealtime insulin is given for a skipped lunch, then dinner has no dose.
- Answer: Dose the meal that is eaten.
- Clue: A skipped meal skips that dose.
- First-line: Match insulin to carbohydrates.
- Trap: Do not dose a meal that was not eaten.
- Source: ADA insulin titration (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Prandial insulin matches intake.

### usmle3-224 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: Discharge after a variceal bleed.
- Answer: Blocker plus a banding plan.
- Clue: Both prevent the next bleed.
- First-line: Schedule bands and start the blocker when stable.
- Trap: Do not discharge on a PPI only.
- Source: AASLD portal hypertension guidance (2017)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Secondary prevention is banding plus a nonselective beta blocker.

### usmle3-225 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: immune system
- Front: CD4 is 250 on treatment and pneumocystis prophylaxis continues.
- Answer: Stop the prophylaxis.
- Clue: The count has recovered.
- First-line: Stop the sulfa used for PCP.
- Trap: Do not stop HIV treatment.
- Source: NIH opportunistic infection guideline (2024)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: PCP prophylaxis stops after CD4 recovery above 200.

### usmle3-226 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A DOAC and a tooth extraction. Kidneys are normal.
- Answer: Do not bridge a DOAC for a tooth.
- Clue: Dental work is low bleeding risk.
- First-line: Time the dose.
- Trap: Do not add enoxaparin.
- Source: ACC periprocedural anticoagulation (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: DOACs are not bridged for dental extraction.

### usmle3-227 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: A patient on CPAP asks about skipping it on vacation.
- Answer: Take the machine.
- Clue: Apnea returns the night it is skipped.
- First-line: Use it every night.
- Trap: Do not skip nights.
- Source: AASM PAP therapy (2019)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Positive airway pressure works only when it is used.

### usmle3-228 · Renal

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: renal and urinary system
- Front: Stage 4 CKD and a magnesium citrate bowel prep.
- Answer: Do not use a magnesium prep.
- Clue: Magnesium accumulates.
- First-line: Use polyethylene glycol.
- Trap: Do not use magnesium citrate.
- Source: FDA bowel prep labeling (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Magnesium preps are avoided in advanced CKD.

### usmle3-229 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: A 60-year-old man who has sex with men has never had a hepatitis C test this decade.
- Answer: Test for hepatitis C.
- Clue: Ongoing risk means more than one lifetime test.
- First-line: Offer it today.
- Trap: Do not assume the birth cohort covered him.
- Source: USPSTF hepatitis C screening (2020)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Ongoing risk is tested periodically.

### usmle3-230 · Behavioral

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: behavioral health
- Front: Lithium level is 0.5 and mania has returned.
- Answer: Raise the level.
- Clue: Mania needs a higher level than quiet maintenance.
- First-line: Increase and recheck.
- Trap: Do not add three drugs first.
- Source: APA bipolar guideline (2002)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Acute mania is treated toward the higher lithium range.

### usmle3-231 · Geriatrics

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: medication use
- Front: Diphenhydramine every night for sleep in an older adult.
- Answer: Stop diphenhydramine.
- Clue: It is a strong anticholinergic.
- First-line: Stop it.
- Trap: Do not switch to another first-generation antihistamine.
- Source: AGS Beers Criteria (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Diphenhydramine is avoided as a hypnotic in older adults.

### usmle3-232 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: LDL is 40 on a high-intensity statin and the patient feels well.
- Answer: Keep the statin.
- Clue: A low LDL is the goal, not toxicity.
- First-line: Continue.
- Trap: Do not cut the dose for the low number.
- Source: AHA/ACC cholesterol guideline (2018)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: A low LDL on treatment is not a reason to stop.

### usmle3-233 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A sulfonylurea and meals that are often skipped.
- Answer: Stop or switch.
- Clue: These drugs release insulin without food.
- First-line: Use a drug that does not do that.
- Trap: Do not keep a sulfonylurea with skipped meals.
- Source: ADA Standards of Care in Diabetes (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Sulfonylureas cause hypoglycemia when meals are missed.

### usmle3-234 · GI

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: gastrointestinal system
- Front: A secure celiac diagnosis and a request for a gluten challenge.
- Answer: Do not challenge.
- Clue: A secure diagnosis is lifelong.
- First-line: Continue the diet.
- Trap: Do not use symptoms as a test.
- Source: ACG celiac guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Confirmed celiac disease is not rechallenged.

### usmle3-235 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: Angina only with hard hills, normal ejection fraction, and medicines that are optimized.
- Answer: Medicines, not a stent first in this setting.
- Clue: A stent is not first for acceptable symptoms.
- First-line: Continue medical therapy.
- Trap: Do not refer for a stent first.
- Source: ACC chronic coronary disease guideline (2023)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Stable coronary disease on therapy is not stent-first care.

### usmle3-236 · Pulmonary

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: respiratory system
- Front: Oxygen saturation is 93 percent at rest in comfortable COPD.
- Answer: No home oxygen yet.
- Clue: The usual threshold is 88 percent.
- First-line: Do not prescribe oxygen.
- Trap: Do not treat 93 percent as qualifying.
- Source: GOLD report (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Resting saturation at or below 88 percent qualifies for long-term oxygen.

### usmle3-237 · Infectious Diseases

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: skin
- Front: A simple abscess was drained and the patient is well on day 3.
- Answer: Drainage was the cure.
- Clue: A long antibiotic course is not required.
- First-line: Stop if any short course is done.
- Trap: Do not prescribe 14 days.
- Source: IDSA skin and soft tissue guideline (2014)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Uncomplicated drained abscesses often need no antibiotics.

### usmle3-238 · Prevention

- Outline: Foundations of independent practice: population health
- Front: Age 50 and never a pneumococcal conjugate vaccine.
- Answer: Give the conjugate vaccine.
- Clue: Fifty is the adult age now.
- First-line: Vaccinate today.
- Trap: Do not defer to 65.
- Source: CDC adult immunization schedule (2025)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Pneumococcal conjugate vaccine is recommended from age 50.

### usmle3-239 · Cardiovascular

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: cardiovascular system
- Front: A patient with HFrEF and atrial fibrillation has a resting rate of 110 on a beta blocker at target.
- Answer: Add digoxin for the rate.
- Clue: The beta blocker stays. Digoxin is the add-on for rate.
- First-line: Add digoxin.
- Trap: Do not stop the beta blocker to use digoxin alone.
- Source: ACC/AHA heart failure guideline (2022)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Digoxin can be added for rate control in HFrEF with atrial fibrillation.

### usmle3-240 · Endocrine

- Outline: Advanced clinical medicine: endocrine system
- Front: A patient on evening basal insulin wakes at 60 and is 210 before breakfast.
- Answer: Lower the evening basal dose.
- Clue: The low at night causes the high morning number.
- First-line: Reduce the night dose.
- Trap: Do not raise the morning dose.
- Source: ADA insulin titration (2026)
- URL: https://www.usmle.org/prepare-your-exam/step-3-materials
- Why this source: Nocturnal hypoglycemia with morning hyperglycemia means too much basal insulin.
